#2688. Fed's John Williams indicates higher interest rates likely 'over time' to address inflation

New York Federal Reserve President John Williams said Monday that policymakers have more work to do to bring inflation under control, though he indicated that further action can unfold over time as the economy continues to grow.

In remarks consistent with previous statements, the influential central banker expressed confidence that inflation will get closer to the Fed's 2% target in 2027 before hitting the goal the following year. He also said that while artificial intelligence is pressuring prices, there's "little evidence" it will be the source of more persistent inflation.

"But getting inflation back to 2% is 100% essential," he said in remarks at the University of Mount Saint Vincent in New York. "We must take actions over time that will ensure that we get inflation back to our 2% goal in a timely manner and on a sustained basis. There is still work to do to get that done."

The comments come at a delicate moment for monetary policy, as the economy continues to grow and inflation stays well above the Fed's goal.

Fed officials have differed over how quickly to raise rates, with some advocating more aggressive action while others, including Williams, have emphasized a measured approach.

Williams ascribed the current inflation pressures to the conflict in Iran, demand for goods associated with the AI build-out and tariffs, the effects of which he said "have largely abated" as a source of pressure. Nevertheless, he expressed confidence that inflation will subside substantially in 2027 even as economic growth remains robust and unemployment edges lower.

The Federal Open Market Committee next meets on Dec. 8-9, with markets fully pricing in a quarter percentage point interest rate increase from the current target range of 3.75%-4%. The remarks from Williams are expected to be the last major policy statement from any Fed official before the December decision.

"Monetary policy cannot move ships or reopen pipelines and refineries, but it can — and must — ensure that these shocks do not lead to broader or persistent inflationary dynamics or erode well-anchored inflation expectations," Williams said, according to his prepared remarks.

Williams did not explicitly address the December meeting expectations, instead pointing to the statement released after the prior FOMC meeting in late October that said the "accumulation of more information" will help decide the future policy path.

"The data that we have received since then show that the economy has remained strong and that inflation has continued to run too high," he said.

#2685. Congress shreds Nvidia's 'outrageous' decision to bring back Huang: 'Failure of character'

Members of Congress from both parties on Monday expressed outrage and incredulity, and doubled down on their calls for investigations, after Nvidia announced it has reinstated Jensen Huang as its president and CEO.

The resurrection of Huang, who had resigned from the artificial intelligence behemoth just 11 days earlier, raises more questions about the company's decision-making and the Trump administration's national security assessments, on top of existing concerns about Nvidia's advanced AI chips being diverted to China, the lawmakers said.

"It's inexplicable to me why they want this guy back as CEO," Rep. Don Bacon, R-Neb., a China hawk who is retiring from Congress, told CNBC in a phone interview. "To me it's a failure of character."

President Donald Trump told CNBC in an extended interview later Monday morning that he personally intervened to convince Nvidia's board to bring Huang back, saying, "I made the deal."

Bacon, chairman of the House Subcommittee on Cyber, Information Technologies, and Innovation, said he wants the next Congress to conduct thorough oversight, while questioning the Trump administration's motives.

"Somebody's responsible for this, and it may be" Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick, whose department is responsible for setting U.S. export controls on AI chips, Bacon said. "But I think we should get to the bottom of this."

Huang, who is scheduled to testify before multiple committees on Capitol Hill starting Dec. 1, is "going to get high scrutiny in a bipartisan way when he comes next week," Bacon said.

Huang's closed-door appearance before a trio of House committees is on the schedule, as the panels seek answers on the national security implications of Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, MS NOW reported. The Senate banking committee announced after Huang's reinstatement that he will appear for a public hearing Dec. 3.

John Fisher of Trident, a spokesman for Huang, declined to comment on the Nvidia CEO's scheduled appearances.

"He better be there," Rep. Gabe Amo, D-R.I., a member of the House Foreign Affairs Committee, told CNBC about Huang's scheduled Dec. 1 appearance. His reinstatement will only intensify congressional probes into Nvidia and its government connections, Amo predicted.

"I think it frankly broadens the scope of the query, and it makes Howard Lutnick and his dealings — supporting his nepo children's operation at Cantor Fitzgerald — party to inquiry as well as any other administration official that has facilitated a status quo in which chips can be sold to China or Chinese operated intermediaries who don't intend good things for the American people," said Amo, referring to the financial firm run by Lutnick's sons that has business ties to Nvidia.

A Cantor spokesperson didn't comment directly when asked about Amo's comments, saying the firm "holds no material proprietary position in Nvidia" and noting its Nvidia positions "arise primarily from equity swaps executed for clients."

The Commerce Department didn't immediately respond to requests to comment on Bacon's and Amo's remarks.

Nvidia in a statement Monday said it "received input from senior levels of the United States government" and that it is confident that Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk."

David Rudenstine, a constitutional law professor and national security scholar at Cardozo School of Law, said the frenzy around Huang's comeback is secondary to lingering national security concerns."The real responsibility for national security is with the administration and not with the CEO of a private company," Rudenstine told CNBC."You're opposed to China getting the chips. You recognize that the intermediary companies are sending chips to China, and you don't put a stop to it. I don't get it," he said.

The CEO's reinstatement, while celebrated by the White House and in Silicon Valley, led to heightened calls on Capitol Hill to seek answers on AI chip exports to China and what the Trump administration's role has been in facilitating that.

"We are sending high-end computer chips to China, and yet we're saying we want to win the AI war. It's a contradiction, and it's a failure of leadership in the White House," Bacon told CNBC.

Sen. Ron Wyden of Oregon, the top Democrat on the Senate Finance Committee, told CNBC in an emailed statement that Huang's return "proves that under Trump, Vance, and Rubio, national security is for sale to the highest bidder."

"We will all pay the price of letting China plunder America's cutting edge technology. Democrats will be pushing for accountability for anyone in the administration that put corrupt deals over our country's security," Wyden, who is also a Senate Intelligence Committee member, said.

Trump in a Truth Social post Monday added that Attorney General Todd Blanche "has confirmed that the U.S. Government does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

Lawmakers, who have already launched multiple investigations related to Huang, Nvidia and related entities, quickly filed in to question those conclusions.

Sen. Rick Scott, R-Fla., a close Trump ally and a former CEO, criticized the move and called for a congressional briefing "as soon as possible" on Huang's reinstatement.

"Nvidia's board is free to vote however they want, but the federal government owes the American people answers about what actually happened," Scott told CNBC in an emailed statement. "This looks terrible on its face, and it drives home the need for Congress to be informed on the full impact of Jensen Huang's actions on our national security and economy."

Rep. Scott Peters, D-Calif., a member of the House Energy and Commerce Committee, told CNBC that it's still not clear to what degree Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy might have compromised national security.

"The fact that the president of the United States is involved in putting him back in there, presumably because maybe he has stock or he just cares about the stock market, it's really reached a bizarre place," Peters said. "And obviously that man has not learned anything from the election about what people think of him, or he doesn't care."

Monday's move also gave fuel to lawmakers who have already questioned Trump, his administration or Nvidia.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., said in a statement via the Senate Banking Committee, where she is the top Democrat and is expected to become chair in January.

Sen. John Cornyn, R-Texas, more tersely questioned the official assurances that Huang poses no risks to national security.

"No national security risk? Based on what?" he wrote on X.

Rep. Raja Krishnamoorthi, D-Ill., who serves on the House Select Committee on the Chinese Communist Party, in an X post called Nvidia's decision to reinstate Huang "outrageous."

"We still don't know what information Chinese intelligence may have obtained from him, and neither the government nor Nvidia's own board has completed its investigation," wrote Krishnamoorthi, who is also a member of the House Oversight and Intelligence panels.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan, Garrett Downs and Luke Fountain contributed to this report.

Nick Sirianni's Daniel Jones sidestep suggests Eagles signing isn't happening appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

While the Philadelphia Eagles finally got back into the win column for the first time since September by keeping their Philly-based Battle of Pennsylvania win streak alive against the Pittsburgh Steelers, it's hard to put too much of that victory on Joe Milton's shoulders, as his play was arguably a net negative for the team.

#2683. Anthropic was supposed to save the AI trade. What went wrong?

Anthropic's historic IPO — the largest in U.S. history — delivered a big win for the company and its investors. But it failed at another task: reviving animal spirits in the AI trade.

The Nasdaq Composite closed down 2.25% amid a broader market pullback with the S&P 500 declining 2% to 7,175.

Investors had been looking forward to the Anthropic IPO as a "clearing event" — reviving the tech sector after months of choppy trading, the Nvidia espionage scandal, and growing concerns that AI chip sales to Asia are likely to slump as Washington cracks down on potential violations of export controls.

Some of those worries resurfaced Thursday as a short seller, Hunterbrook Media, alleged in a report that firmware sold by chipmaker AMD had turned up in U.S.-blacklisted companies. AMD stock closed down more than 7%. The chipmaker told CNBC it complies with U.S. law.

Adding to the worries, the House Foreign Affairs Committee released documents showing that a subagency of the Commerce Department approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies. That also raised the prospect of an Asia sales slowdown under stricter export controls.

Many investors and analysts continue to express confidence in the AI trade, noting that Anthropic's successful market debut — the stock closed up 17% from its IPO price and raised more than $81 billion — bodes well for capital spending across tech.

But investors acknowledged that this is a high-tension moment and said it's time to be more strategic about the trade.

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, described himself as a "circumspect bull," making the comparison to market conditions in 2022 when the big indices tanked in the back half of the year.

"You could have still made money that year. You just needed to have the right combination of longs and shorts," he said.

Part of the challenge, Niles said, has been a price war between the two cutting-edge frontier labs, Anthropic and OpenAI. That has posed a threat to the overall revenue and profit potential of AI and the capex going into it. Fierce competition on price is producing collective losses between the two top companies, he notes.

"The combined revenue growth for OpenAI plus Anthropic went from growing 75% sequentially in Q2 to 48% in Q3," he told CNBC. "The combined losses went from $7.9 billion to about $8.4 billion from Q2 to Q3."

While uncertainty is high now, some resolution may be on the horizon if a greater degree of commercial and regulatory clarity emerges over the next few months.

Trading experts also told CNBC that the tech sector's troubles Thursday were a more serious matter than Anthropic simply siphoning capital from other parts of the market.

"There's a reset of AI in general," said JJ Kinahan, senior vice president at Cboe Global Markets.

Investors for years have profited in tech by buying the dip, he said, but "there's been an attitude change that [you] can't buy things blindly and they'll all go up." Nvidia's ongoing declines have shaken confidence in the buy-the-dip strategy, he said.

Anthropic's next few trading days will be a key gauge of the market's strength. Friday will be a "wild card," Kinahan said. It could be a "final flush out" for the market, or a bounce that leaves the market in "no man's land" while volatility remains elevated.

A better gauge may be Monday, when options on Anthropic start trading, providing insight into whether retail investors will back the company in force — providing more of a vote of confidence in the AI trade.

"If people think we're going up [on Anthropic] they'll buy stock, but retail particularly will just buy calls," he said. "There's a bigger test coming Monday."

For now, investors continue to sell AI-related stocks.

Neoclouds, the generally highly indebted AI-dedicated computing providers at the outer edges of the AI trade, were down substantially Thursday, with CoreWeave closing down more than 6%.

Hyperscalers such as Alphabet and Amazon.com, viewed as relatively defensive AI bets, also finished lower.

One bright spot: Cybersecurity stocks continued to rally, with Palo Alto Networks and CrowdStrike both closing up. AI is finding security holes across software infrastructure, creating a scramble to patch the vulnerabilities before hackers can exploit them.

— CNBC's Daren Fonda contributed reporting.

#2681. Peterson: Here’s what Bessent’s about-face on Jensen Huang reveals about Trump and China

For a minute there, it seemed like someone in the Trump administration might actually be willing to stand up to China.

For about a week and a half, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent was the face of an emerging harder line on Nvidia over the potential risks its co-founder Jensen Huang's affairs posed to national security. Bessent was giving voice to China hawks who had felt sidelined by the Trump administration's willingness to sell artificial intelligence chips to  China.

They see China's AI program as a major threat, and want to restrain its growth by cutting off the supply of advanced chips to China. The resignation of Huang, who pressed the Trump Administration to allow the exports, seemed to signal that the hawks had found new influence in Washington.

By this weekend, Bessent had softened his public resistance to Huang's reinstatement. That came just in time for Nvidia's board to reappoint Huang as CEO at President Donald Trump's urging.

Whether any real damage was done to national security isn't clear. But it is safe to say that the incoming Democratic majority in Congress won't take Trump at his word when he said on CNBC Monday that the Department of Justice "did a pretty good investigation" that exonerated Huang.

Congress will look deeply into the worries Bessent himself raised about Huang's acknowledged romantic relationship with a woman The New York Times and others have reported was a Chinese intelligence asset.

Nvidia's board said it had received assurances from top U.S. officials and had concluded his return wasn't a national security risk.

A top concern is whether China was able to use the affair to help divert Nvidia's high-end artificial intelligence chips toward helping its military. Bessent in a Nov. 13 interview with CNBC outlined the stakes.

"Any leakage is very frustrating because it hurts the — it hurts our great companies and in the AI race it gives the Chinese an advantage," he said.

Bessent said that his department and others had wanted to blacklist Megaspeed, the company previously run by Alice Huang, Jensen Huang's paramour. But "Commerce has the pen," Bessent said.

The Commerce Department is headed by Secretary Howard Lutnick, Bessent's rival.

Putting Huang back at Nvidia wasn't originally Bessent's preference. He said in a Fox interview on Nov. 15 that the board should "go outside the company" to find a new leader. But by this past weekend, Bessent had apparently gotten on board with Trump's wishes.

"Jensen is on the outside now," Bessent told CBS on Sunday, in trying to explain his apparent policy shift.

Commerce has since taken steps to tighten some restrictions meant to prevent U.S. chips from helping China advance in the AI race.

But there is bipartisan worry about the Nvidia episode. "I think we need to catalog the damage that's been done," Sen. Tom Cotton, R-Ark., told The Wall Street Journal. He said Lutnick had not responded to letters he sent saying the administration should be "taking the threats of communist China more seriously."

But Trump has clearly put an upper limit on how aggressively anyone in his administration can push back on China. The president said Monday that he was swayed by the pageantry in his visit to China last week.

Xi "met me at the plane, which is something they've never done, which was a sign," Trump said.

Trump said he felt like he needed to intervene to back Huang because the stock market was plummeting in the wake of Nvidia's troubles — a development he also blamed on Democrats' midterm victory.

"I watched our country lose $5 trillion because of a bad election," he said. "If I didn't get involved, you'd probably have a very sad Nvidia for a long time to come." Now, its stock performance will be "unbelievable."

Bessent on Sunday played down the market risks. The company "continues to be fantastically profitable, and there is near unlimited demand for their chips worldwide," he told CBS.

Lutnick has embraced Trump's transactional urges. He has backed deals in which the U.S. takes equity stakes in companies such as Intel, saying the taxpayer deserves some of the upside.

Lutnick in January said he had heard Huang pitch Trump on allowing Nvidia to sell its relatively more advanced H200 chips into China.

"There are strong — two sides. We shouldn't sell them any or we should sell them." Where does he stand? "Right in the middle," Lutnick told Bloomberg. Trump decided to go ahead with the deal.

Bessent has said he is "neither a hawk nor a dove" on China, and is simply trying to manage the ultra-sensitive relationship between the world's two biggest economies. But the upshot of the Huang affair seems to be that Bessent wasn't able to sway the president on a matter that seemed cut-and-dried just days ago.

Trump said he discussed whether Huang should return to Nvidia with Lutnick, Bessent and Vice President JD Vance. "Howard Lutnick was very good. Very, he's very professional," Trump said on CNBC Monday.

"I call them the opposites, Scott and Howard," Trump added, before declaring them "both good."

#2680. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2678. Cathie Wood’s Ark Invest was a big buyer of Anthropic as she tells CNBC she’s ‘quite optimistic’

Cathie Wood was a major buyer of Anthropic following its debut Thursday on the Nasdaq, adding shares in all her major funds.

Wood's firm, which already owned private shares of the artificial intelligence company in its Ark Venture Fund leading up to the initial public offering, bought $90.8 million worth of Anthropic in six funds, according to a trade notification from the firm.

"We've owned Anthropic since early '23," Wood told CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Friday. "It's our best performing equity by far in ARKVX, our venture fund. ... Our confidence has grown with time as it really took over the enterprise" AI business.

Anthropic shares jumped 17% in their first day, but closed well off the highs of the session and were headed for sizable losses Friday. The Nasdaq, which was expected to get a boost from a major IPO reviving enthusiasm about the technology trade, fell by 2% on Thursday.

"We're climbing a wall of worry," said Wood. "Everybody is worried about AI hype and the political situation after the election, but I think we'll look back and say, OK this is more like the '80s and '90s, climbing a wall of worry you know with bricks much like what we have today: inflation, interest rates, wars, new technologies and so forth so quite optimistic here."

The Anthropic stakes are relatively small across the funds, but Wood typically will buy shares in companies she likes steadily during long stretches. Wood said she is "averaging down" with her purchases of Anthropic.

In her $6.1 billion Ark Innovation ETF, Anthropic is a 0.3% stake. The biggest holdings in that fund are Tesla and SpaceX, representing more than 21% of the fund together.

Ark Innovation, which retail investors piled into during the pandemic as it gained about 150% in 2020, tumbled the subsequent next two years, losing 67% in 2022 alone. Wood's following was dented after the big losses.

The fund had a comeback stretch as the bull market revved back up from 2023 to 2025, but has fallen on hard times again this year, down more than 10% in 2026. The S&P 500 is up 4.8% this year.

Wood dismissed comparisons of today's environment to the dot-com bubble.

"Back then if an Anthropic had gone public, it probably would have gone up threefold the first day," she said. "That's how crazy things were."

#2676. Sixers' record-setting wait for the West ends against Trail Blazers

Sixers' record-setting wait for the West ends against Trail Blazers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Have you noticed anything different about the Sixers' season?

No, not the fact that LeBron James and Jaylen Brown are wearing red, white and blue and have helped Philadelphia build an Eastern Conference-leading 14-3 record.

The difference is hidden in their schedule. All 17 games have come against Eastern Conference opponents.

When the Sixers welcome the Portland Trail Blazers to Xfinity Mobile Arena on Monday night, it will mark the latest they have ever gone into a season before facing a Western Conference team.

Philadelphia's 18-game wait also ties the 1979-80 Atlanta Hawks for the second latest by an Eastern Conference team. Only the 1980-81 Milwaukee Bucks went longer, meeting the West for the first time in their 21st game.

The Sixers have played who has been placed in front of them and won five consecutive games. They have already beaten the Knicks twice and own victories over Cleveland and Detroit.

Their first Western Conference matchup introduces a different comparison.

The West entered Monday with 11 teams above .500. The East had seven. Western Conference teams were also 41-20 against the East through Sunday.

Portland will not answer every question about how the Sixers compare with the other conference. It begins a more varied portion of their schedule.

The Sixers will face Memphis and Denver on consecutive nights next week. Dallas and Houston visit later in December before Philadelphia travels to play the Lakers, Clippers, Kings and Suns over a six-day stretch.

Portland is 10-6 under first-year head coach Micah Nori, the father of Phillies prospect Dante Nori.

The Blazers are averaging 116.1 points while allowing 110.3 per game, with both figures ranking in the top 10. Their 15.4 made three-pointers per game rank second in the NBA.

Deni Avdija leads Portland with 23.9 points per game. Damian Lillard is averaging 20.6 points in his return to the franchise, while 7-foot-2 center Donovan Clingan has supplied size around a deep group of guards and wings.

Clingan posted 22 points and 13 rebounds Saturday night at Madison Square Garden. Portland erased a 26-point deficit and briefly took the lead before Mikal Bridges made a game-winning three-pointer at the buzzer.

The 97-96 loss dropped the Blazers to 1-3 over their last four games. Slow starts have followed them during that stretch.

"Our last three games, we haven't gotten off to great starts, and that's just something that we got to correct," Lillard said. "Our energy and our focus and our intent has to be better from the jump. We can't let this trend continue."

The Sixers know something about overcoming early deficits. Six of their 14 victories have required double-digit comebacks.

Portland will be missing two major pieces. Ja Morant is out with a left hamstring strain, while Shaedon Sharpe remains sidelined following right meniscus surgery.

Even without Morant, the Blazers can turn to Lillard, Jrue Holiday and Scoot Henderson to initiate their offense. Avdija and Toumani Camara provide size on the perimeter, while Clingan gives Portland the kind of interior presence that has occasionally troubled the Sixers.

Anfernee Simons and Jabari Walker know the Portland organization better than any other Sixers.

The two shared the floor for 128 games with the Trail Blazers. Portland won 43 of them.

Their responsibilities have changed in Philadelphia. So have the expectations.

Simons scored 18 points in each of the Sixers' last two games and made seven three-pointers. He started in place of Tyrese Maxey against Orlando and hit the clinching three in his hometown. Three nights later, he supplied four more threes off the bench in the Sixers' 132-129 win over Milwaukee.

Walker has also found steady work during the Sixers' recent frontcourt injuries. He recorded nine points, seven rebounds and four assists against the Bucks after scoring 11 against Indiana and hitting a go-ahead three late in Orlando.

Both players recently discussed how their difficult seasons in Portland changed their perspective on filling smaller roles for a winning team.

"You feel appreciative of the times where you go into a situation where your role might be a little bit less, but you're winning," Simons said. "That's something that you can't take for granted, for sure, because it's hard to win in the NBA. We've seen it firsthand."

Their former team will now see them operating within a deeper rotation than the one Philadelphia carried through much of the past week.

Brown, Dean Wade and Adem Bona were all listed as probable on the Sixers' initial injury report after missing time.

Brown and Wade have each missed the last two games. Bona has been sidelined since Nov. 4 because of a cervical spine sprain. Dominick Barlow returned Saturday and scored 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting in his season debut.

Joel Embiid was added to the injury report Monday morning as questionable because of an illness. His availability will determine how the Sixers approach Clingan and Robert Williams III inside.

The Sixers have leaned on smaller lineups and reserve contributors while Nurse has managed injuries across the roster. Monday could give him more options, even if Embiid cannot play.

FanDuel Sportsbook listed the Sixers as 3.5-point favorites Monday afternoon. Philadelphia was -152 on the moneyline, Portland was +128 and the over-under was 229.5.

The Blazers are 7-4 on the road. The Sixers are 7-2 at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

After 17 games within their own conference, the Sixers will finally see what their early success looks like against the other side.

#2673. Cam Skattebo is not a John Harbaugh guy: Rex Ryan

Cam Skattebo is having a hard time selling Rex Ryan.

The Giants rusher celebrated his game-clinching first-down run during Sunday's 23-18 win over the Jaguars with a backflip, days after saying he was done with the move. It didn't sit well with Ryan — and he doesn't think it endears him to Big Blue coach John Harbaugh, either.

"Truth be known, this is not a John Harbaugh-type guy," Ryan told ESPN New York's "DiPietro & Rothenberg" on Monday. "I'm telling you. Now, his toughness, that's a John Harbaugh-type player. Cam Skattebo #44 of the New York Giants runs the ball against the Jaguars. Corey Sipkin for the NY POST

The ex-Jets coach also referenced Skattebo's third-quarter fumble — his fifth of the season — that put Jacksonville on offense at the Giants' 31-yard line.

"I mean, golly, for a big, powerful back, I like this kid, I love the way he plays: physical, downhill type guy, all those type of things, is impressive," Ryan said. This is an issue, this is an absolute issue."

Harbaugh sent his top rusher a message after he accrued four fumbles in the first five games of the season, giving him zero touches in a 34-27 loss to the Saints in Week 6. The coach hasn't hidden his want to upgrade the running back room, hoping to land Jeremiyah Love in the draft and then engaging in trade talks for Jonathan Taylor. Neither came to fruition, and for now, Skattebo remains atop the depth chart.

"The fact that they came back to me and trusted me at the end of the game to close it out shows you how much they trust me," he said Sunday. "And I give love and props and support and I appreciate them for that."

#2671. Observations after Sixers survive Bucks' late surge, reach 14-3

Observations after Sixers survive Bucks' late surge, reach 14-3 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers survived the Bucks' late surge and reached 14-3 on the season Saturday afternoon with a 132-129 win at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Tyler Herro nearly sent the game to overtime with a buzzer-beating three-pointer, but replays showed that he released his shot late.

Herro had a massive performance for the 1-16 Bucks, posting a career-high 50 points on 15-for-23 shooting, 11 rebounds and seven assists.

Joel Embiid had 26 points, a season-high 17 rebounds and nine assists for the Sixers.

LeBron James (21 points), Tyrese Maxey (17 points) and Anfernee Simons (18) were among the seven Sixers to score in double figures.

The Sixers were down Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain), Dean Wade (left hamstring tightness/soreness), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear).

Next up for the Sixers is a Monday night meeting with the Trail Blazers. Here are observations on their victory over the Bucks:

Maxey returned to the Sixers' starting five after missing the team's comeback win Wednesday over the Magic with right foot soreness. He was joined by VJ Edgecombe, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

James threw down a dunk on the day's first play. Embiid then dished to a cutting Edgecombe for a layup that put the Sixers up 4-0.

The Bucks quickly found a foothold in the game.

Embiid missed his first four shots, including two that were swatted by Myles Turner. Maxey began 1 for 5 from the floor. Herro canned a three-pointer to give Milwaukee a 15-8 lead.

The Sixers entered the game with a minus-13.2 net rating in first quarters, which ranked 28th in the NBA. They faced a 30-29 deficit after Saturday's first period.

Even with Maxey available again, Labaron Philon Jr. deservingly stayed in the Sixers' rotation following an excellent outing in Orlando.

He scored two points in six first-half minutes. The 20-year-old made a rookie mistake at the tail end of the first quarter, fouling Herro on a three-point attempt.

Dominick Barlow checked in for his first action of the season with 2:51 remaining in the first quarter. He'd been sidelined for the Sixers' first 16 games because of a lower back stress reaction.

Barlow scored his first points early in the second quarter when he tipped in a Maxey miss. He then teamed up with James in crowd-pleasing style, sprinting ahead of the pack on a fast break and flushing home the 22-time All-Star's long-distance lob.

The afternoon was not devoid of rust for Barlow. Shortly after that alley-oop, he grabbed an offensive rebound, dribbled out to the perimeter and fired an open three-pointer … which he air balled short.

To Barlow's credit, he took another three early in the fourth quarter and drained it. He logged 11 minutes and was quite productive, tallying 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting and four rebounds.

"It felt great," Barlow said of his three. "Got to shoot the ball with confidence. … I've obviously got to get a game feel; I've been missing that. But I worked too hard (in the offseason) to not shoot the ball when I'm open."

Kyle Kuzma hit a three late in the second quarter to build the Bucks' lead to 59-52. After Sixers head coach Nick Nurse's timeout, the team closed the first half on a 8-0 run featuring an Embiid dunk.

The Sixers went just 3 for 16 from long range in the first half, but their low-turnover ways helped mitigate those shooting struggles.

They committed a single turnover in the first half and finished with four giveaways. That's tied for their lowest ever on record in a single game. The Sixers have the NBA's lowest turnover percentage outside of garbage time, according to Cleaning the Glass.

"No matter who you're playing against, teams try to turn you over, they try to speed you up," James said. "Us taking care of the ball, that's a lot of the reason why we won this game."

The Sixers' shooters were bound to start sinking some open looks eventually. Simons, Caldwell-Pope and Edgecombe all made threes late in the third quarter and the team's lead grew as high as 13 points.

Thanks largely to Herro's individuals exploits, the Bucks stayed competitive. AJ Green buried a corner three to cut Milwaukee's deficit to 106-103 with 8:10 to go in the game.

James sought out his shot much more following the Sixers' timeout and did so with great success. He scored four buckets inside over the next four minutes, including a big slam that put the Sixers up 121-112.

It appeared the main questions left were whether Embiid could reach a triple-double and just how many points Herro would rack up.

However, the Bucks forced the Sixers to work down the stretch. Herro got to the 50-point milestone and trimmed the Sixers' lead to two points when he converted a layup with 19.2 seconds left.

The Sixers safely navigated to the final buzzer by the slimmest of margins. Embiid made four late free throws and everyone on the Sixers' sideline was relieved to see that Herro hadn't quite released his jumper in time.

"I try not to complain too much about any win in this league," Nurse said. "The message is we've got to finish a little better."

#2670. Las Vegas Grand Prix Data Shows Wild Chaos And Missed Wins

The 2026 Las Vegas Grand Prix turned into a chaotic mess where drivers swapped places constantly. The teams and drivers fought for the lead on nearly every single lap. Max Verstappen lost the top spot to George Russell on lap two but took it back right away. The official lap chart only counts a lead change when it sticks, so Russell did not get the lead until lap 28 after the Safety Car came out. This back-and-forth action made the race feel like a yo-yo that never stopped moving.

Isack Hadjar proved that breaking free from the pack leads to the fastest speeds. After he pitted for a new front wing, he set the fastest lap time for eight straight laps. Liam Lawson from Racing Bulls actually posted the quickest lap of the entire event with a time of 1 minute and 35.591 seconds. His average speed hit 229.73 kph, which was faster than anyone else on the track.

Mercedes had a chance to win but lost it due to a late mistake by George Russell. He passed Antonelli on the second-to-last lap but locked up his tires and hit the barriers. This crash dropped him from third place all the way down to ninth place on the final standings list. The team felt they had a shot at the win before that crash happened.

Antonelli tried to save the day but ran out of battery power at the worst possible time. He managed to pass the two McLaren cars and Pierre Gasly to run in third place before the final laps. He then passed his teammate Russell until that crash occurred. On the last lap, he tried to use his energy to attack Verstappen but had almost no power left. He finished fourth, just holding off Gasly. Andrew Shovlin, Trackside Engineering Director, noted, "We know we could have done a better job today and we will look at those moments, learn from them and aim to finish the final two races of the season more strongly than we did today."

The race finished with a change in the top three positions. Oscar Piastri and Lando Norris from McLaren capitalized on the chaos to secure their spots on the podium. Piastri recorded a fastest lap of 1:36.334 while Norris posted a time of 1:36.182. The data confirms that the slipstream effect was massive, making the order change constantly throughout the night. Drivers had to be very smart with their energy and positioning to survive the night.

George Russell admitted he regretted nothing even though the result was painful for the team. He felt the move to pass Antonelli was necessary to fight for the win. The Italian driver did well to hold off Gasly and stay in fourth place despite the energy issues. Formula 1 Official sources confirm that there are two races left in the season for teams to finish strongly.

#2669. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

#2668. What Alijah Vera-Tucker returning to practice means for the Patriots

The New England Patriots returned to work on Monday, officially ending their bye week. When they did, they saw a member of their starting offensive line make his comeback: left guard Alijah Vera-Tucker participated in practice for the first time since suffering a hamstring injury in Week 6.

Vera-Tucker's return means that he has now officially been designated to return from injured reserve and is eligible to rejoin New England's active roster as soon as this week's game against the Los Angeles Chargers. Let's find out how this impacts the Patriots.

The actual on-field impact of Vera-Tucker returning to the 53-man roster will be discussed once he is actually activated. For now, however, let's focus on the return designation itself and it's impact on player and team.

First of all, the timeline of his return. At the moment, Vera-Tucker remains on injured reserve albeit with the "designated for return" attachment next to his status. This signifies that his window to come back has now opened and will remain so for the next 21 days. He can be activated at any point during those three weeks, and if not would revert to regular IR for the remainder of the season.

Obviously, with return designations limited — more on those in a second — the expectation is that the 27-year-old will return to the 53-man roster during that 21-day period. That activation might happen as early as Friday, when the Patriots are set to fly to L.A. for their game against the Chargers.

The Patriots currently have 53 players on their active roster, the maximum allowed by the NFL during the regular season. This means that whenever the team decides to actually activate Vera-Tucker, a follow-up move will become necessary.

What that move will look like is anybody's guess at this point in time. It could be as simple as cutting a player from the roster, possibly from an offensive line that would be 11 players deep once Vera-Tucker is fully back.

Vera-Tucker has extensive starting experience at both left and right guard, but the belief is that he will return to his previous position in the Patriots lineup when all is said and done. While this should help solidify the left guard spot — he looked solid in his first six games of the season — the right guard position is where the real intrigue now starts.

With Mike Onwenu sidelined and increasingly unlikely to return at any point this year, the Patriots realistically have three starting options available: Greg Van Roten, Ben Brown and Matt Pryor. Van Roten was the next man up at right guard after Onwenu broke his ankle in Week 2; Brown took over for him when he went to IR one game later; Pryor has started the last four games.

Having Vera-Tucker back allows the Patriots to use their interior O-line resources differently, and the process of doing so is already getting started this week.

Every NFL team is allowed to bring up to eight players back from injured reserve during the regular season, and Vera-Tucker has now taken the sixth of those spots.

Previously, the Patriots designated wide receivers A.J. Brown and Mack Hollins, fullback Reggie Gilliam, and offensive linemen Marcus Bryan and Van Roten for activation. All of them have since made their way to the 53-man roster, but even if they hadn't their return spots would still count against the eight available.

This also means that the Patriots have only two spots remaining to be used on a group of players consisting of RB TreVeyon Henderson, WR Romeo Doubs, RG Mike Onwenu, ED Quintayvious Hutchins, LB Khalil Jacobs and S Dell Pettus. QB Drake Maye, who is expected to miss at least six weeks after fracturing his fibula is not even part of that group; he remains on the active roster for now.

His future is uncertain. What seems like a safe bet, on the other hand, is that the seventh returnee will be Romeo Doubs.

#2667. Klint Kubiak on Fernando Mendoza: With two starts against two elite defenses, I've seen improvement

While Fernando Mendoza is still looking for his first win as a starting quarterback, the No. 1 overall pick of this year’s draft displayed his considerable potential in Sunday’s loss to the Broncos.

Las Vegas was able to come back from down 17-0 to tie the game midway through the third quarter. Mendoza then engineered another touchdown drive midway through the fourth period to pull the Raiders within seven.

But the comeback effort ended when Mendoza threw a pick-six to Ja’Quan McMillian with just under two minutes left in regulation.

Head coach Klint Kubiak told reporters on Monday that he saw Mendoza take steps in multiple areas.

“No. 1 is just the environment,” Kubiak said, via Sam Warren of TheAthletic.com. “That’s a very hostile environment, and I thought, overall, he did a good job of getting everybody on the same page. I thought he used his legs extremely well, extended some plays.

“For his second start in the NFL against two elite defenses, I’ve seen improvement.”

Kubiak also complimented Mendoza for the way he was able to bring the team back from being down 17.

“That’s who he is. That’s what he’s made of,” Kubiak said. “I think back to us being backed up, him taking a sack and then him making a really big third-down throw to Jack Bech. That’s what you need from your quarterback.”

Mendoza finished Sunday’s game 18-of-37 for 267 yards with three touchdowns and one interception. Mendoza’s next game will be his primetime debut, as the Raiders will face the Browns on Sunday night in Week 12.

#2666. James sees a lot to like — and improve — for East-leading Sixers

James sees a lot to like — and improve — for East-leading Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James likes to refer to the NBA season as a "marathon."

A few miles in, the Sixers lead the Eastern Conference pack.

With 17 regular-season contests down of 82, the team sits at 14-3, which is 2.5 games ahead of the second-seeded Celtics. The Sixers squeaked out their latest win, a 132-129 decision Saturday over the cellar-dwelling Bucks, but the bottom line is they've become accustomed to finding many routes to victory.

"We want to build habits," James said. "We want to try to take steps forward every game that we get the opportunity. I know sometimes it doesn't happen that way, but we want to do it.

"We want to strive to be as great as we can offensively, defensively and on special teams. A win is a win, but there's ways to win and ways to try to continue to build habits over the course of a marathon."

All sorts of Sixers games have gone down to the wire and they've won every single one.

The team is an incredible 12-0 in clutch situations, defined as games with a point differential of five or under in the final five minutes. The Sixers rank third in the NBA in clutch offensive rating (140.3) and third in clutch net rating (plus-42.0).

"It's good to build that part of our game," James said. "Obviously, we wanted to close the game out a lot better. But a team like that, we know they're going to be scrappy. They're going to play as hard as they can for 48 minutes. … We can learn from that."

James was the centerpiece of the Sixers' fourth-quarter offense Saturday. He scored 13 of his 21 points between the 7:46 and 3:13 mark of the fourth.

The four-time MVP got two buckets on no-nonsense drives and another on an after-timeout isolation hoop over rookie Brayden Burries. James also scored three times on a designed Horns action where he brought the ball up, used Tyrese Maxey's back screen at the elbow and caused serious problems for Milwaukee in the paint.

"We continue to add things to the half-court offense and also to the late-game package," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "That's something we added a few game ago. It's one of those things where, especially if we're in the bonus, we've got some big bodies that can cut down the lane and have feel for timing. It was working. Just keep going to it."

James has grown in comfort with Nurse's coaching style. He knows the Sixers expect him to be a de facto coach on the floor, too.

"I'm just trying to be there for the team in any capacity they want," he said. "Just trying to be an extension of his calls and his commands out there on the floor. Trying to make his life and his job a little bit easier as well.

"But it's been great, not only with him but the whole coaching staff. They've welcomed me with open arms and allowed me to kind of be the voice for the team every day, and take that responsibility."

After a tremendous first impression defensively and some subpar offensive nights, the Sixers' numbers have recently evened out.

The team is 12th in offensive rating outside of garbage time and 12th in defensive rating, per Cleaning the Glass. In essence, the Sixers have been a bit above average on both ends of the floor and downright brilliant in the closing minutes.

It's all added up to an early lead in a very long race.

"I think we've bought into the pass," James said. "We have so many guys that can score the ball at elite levels. … In order for us to be a super-complete team, I think the power of the pass is very infectious. And it's also key for our other guys, to make sure they feel in a rhythm and they feel confident. We've done that from Day 1 and I think it's gotten better and better. … And I think our defense has been really good for the majority of the season.

"(Today) it wasn't so great, but we've been very good defensively throughout the course of the season. … I don't want to say there's anything bad. There's so many things for us to do better for sure. There's games where we can defensive rebound a lot better and there's games where we can not turn the ball over (as much). And there's games where we can be a little more phsyical defensively at the point of attack like (today).

"I wouldn't say anything has been bad, though. That's a good thing. We want to continue to have room for improvement and we don't want to be great in November. That would be weird."

#2665. FSU hires Georgia DC Glenn Schumann as head coach

The Florida State Seminoles have finally made their decision. According to a report from Pete Thamel and Andrea Adlenson of ESPN, they have hired Georgia Bulldogs' defensive coordinator Glenn Schumann to be their next head coach.

The deal is reportedly for five years. According to Thamel, Schumann is expected to stay with Georgia through the end of the regular season and postseason. The Bulldogs are currently the No. 1 team in the country and primed for anothr deep run in the College Football Playoffs.

Once FSU fired Mike Norvell, a plethora of names were linked to the head coaching vacancy in Tallahassee. Jake Dickert of the Wake Forest Demon Deacons, Manny Diaz of the Duke Blue Devils, and even former Seminoles' head coach Jimbo Fisher were names that were floating around. However, it became clear over the last few days that Schumann was the focus, especially after FSU met with him on Sunday.

He began his coaching career as a student assistant with the Alabama Crimson Tide in 2008 and kept that role through 2011 when he graduated with a Bachelor of Arts degree. After that, he was a graduate assistant at Alabama through 2014, earning a Master's degree in Sports Management in 2013. He was also the Director of Football Operations from 2014-2015.

Schumann worked closely with former Alabama coach Nick Saban and Kirby Smart, who was the defensive coordinator there at the time, helping install the weekly defensive game plan for the Crimson Tide. He was apart of four SEC championship teams and four national championship teams at Alabama.

When Smart was hired as the head coach at Georgia in 2016, Schumann came along as the inside linebackers coach, a position he held through the 2018 season. From 2019 to 2023, Schumann kept his position as the inside linebackers coach, but he was also the co-defensive coordinator. From 2024 to the present, Schumann has been the defensive coordinator and inside linebackers coach at Georgia.

The Bulldogs have had unbelievable success since Schumann came to Athens along with Smart. They've won four SEC titles and two national championships over that span. Since Schumann has held the co-defensive coordinator title or been the head guy, Georgia has been excellent defensively. They were second and fifth in total defense in 2021 and 2022 while winning back-to-back national titles. They were ninth in 2023, 30th in 2024, 14th in 2025, and they're sixth in total defense in 2026.

Schumann is from Valdosta, Georgia. That's in the southern part of the state, which is prime recruiting grounds for Florida State. Recruiting has been problematic for FSU for quite some time, and the hope is that Schumann can help immensely with that. Not just with his ties to southern Georgia, but with the overall recruiting process and all the connections he's made throughout his career.

Florida State will now look to build a staff around Schumann and get the program back to national relevancy. A new era in Seminoles' football starts now.

#2664. 49ers release WR Brandon Aiyuk

They waited as long as they could to do what they knew all along they’d be doing.

Two weeks after 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk was reinstated from the reserve/left squad list, the 49ers have released him.

The 49ers had received a two-week roster exemption upon Aiyuk’s reinstatement. Under the Collective Bargaining Agreement, the 49ers owed him no pay for the duration of the roster exemption.

He’ll now go to waivers, and he undoubtedly will be unclaimed. After that, he’ll become a free agent.

Many expect Aiyuk to sign with the Commanders, possibly with a practice-squad stint the first step back to the field.

Where he goes after that remains to be seen. Multiple contenders could use help at the position, if Aiyuk can perform like he did before the 2024 ACL tear that ultimately derailed his career.

#2660. Lapel players, coaches soaking up state finals moment

LAPEL — For the first time in program history, Lapel's football team will play for the state championship when they face top-ranked and defending Class 1A state champion South Putnam at Lucas Oil Stadium at 11 a.m. Friday morning.

They players know what is in front of them and are focused on playing for their town and teammates. They are a focus and goal-oriented group.

But that does not mean they won't take a moment at practice Wednesday or before Friday's kickoff to enjoy the moment.

"We're going to soak in all of it," senior defensive back Reed Hamilton said. "This is our last game (together) ever."

Coach Tim Miller smiles at the though of reprising Gene Hackman's scene in Hoosiers when he measured the height of the rim at Hinkle Fieldhouse and showed them it was the same as their tiny gym in the fictional town of Hickory.

He believes his kids will understand that 100 yards in Lapel equals 100 yards in Indianapolis.

"Wednesday (practice) is as much about letting them soak that in and knowing they are playing where the Colts play," he said. "For any life experiences – a vacation, a hike, whatever – it's important that you soak in those moments. Life isn't always lived in Facebook, you have to experience it. They've earned the trip, I hope they soak in every minute of it."

For 24 Lapel seniors, this game is the culmination of a lifelong pursuit. It is born out of the hard work in the weight room, during weekly practices during the season and offseason workouts that started not long after the last season ended.

But they have earned the right to play in the home of the NFL's Indianapolis Colts as well as the right to soak up the experience.

It is also, win or lose, the final time two of the team's seniors will play for their coach fathers.

Running back and linebacker Jack Miller is Tim's oldest son and will play at Taylor University next season. While Tim's son Tate is a freshman on the team and his daughter Drew is one of the team's student managers, knowing that the last time he will share the field with Jack will come at Lucas Oil Stadium makes the moment even more special.

"For me personally, it was definitely the perfect year to come down here, my wife was pretty happy," Tim said Monday. "It's been nothing short of awesome and, in Jack's case, being able to coach him for four years has been an absolute honor and a pleasure.

"My wife's been a wreck, not going to lie about that. It'll be emotional and, even now I'm getting emotional about it. At the end of the day, win or lose, he gets to end his high school career at Lucas Oil Stadium."

Page's father Larry is an assistant for the Bulldogs as well — he coaches Mason's position, defensive back — and Mason plans on savoring that experience as well before he departs for Ouachita Baptist University in Arkadelphia, Arkansas to continue his own football career next year.

"I was driving by the football field last night and I was thinking, 'Dang, I played my last game there last Friday,'" Mason said. "At the same time, it's crazy that all the work we've put on that field, we get to bring here this Friday. That's what I told my dad, all that hard work has paid off in this game."

Tim points out that he did not coach his kids prior to their arrival at the high school level, crediting the town's Optimist Club program for developing the young talent in town.

But these four years have been just as special for his son.

"Mom was crying about it this weekend," Jack said. "It's kind of weird thinking about it that, no matter what, this is my last time being coached by him."

#2659. Bears have gone five straight games without ever trailing for first time since 1985

The Bears' win over the Saints on Sunday was their fifth consecutive game taking a first-quarter lead and winning without ever trailing. That gives this Bears team an accomplishment the franchise hasn't achieved in 41 years.

The last Bears team to win five straight games without ever trailing was the 1985 Bears, one of the most dominant teams in NFL history.

That 1985 Bears team lost just one game, a Monday night classic against the Dolphins in the 13th game of the season, and after that loss to the Dolphins they never trailed in the final three games of the regular season, or in the two playoff games they won to earn a trip to Super Bowl XX. In the Super Bowl, the Bears fell behind 3-0 in the first quarter before winning 46-10, snapping their streak of five straight games without trailing.

This Bears team isn't dominating quite like that 1985 team, but the Bears are currently on a very impressive run: They haven't just won five games in a row, but they've scored in every quarter of all five games, the franchise's first streak of 20 consecutive quarters scoring since the 1941 Bears. That team, like the 1985 team, won the NFL championship.

The 2026 Bears are playing like some of the best teams in franchise history. They're jumping out to early leads and never looking back.

#2658. Prediction markets outlook for stocks in 2026 dims amid AI sell-off

As stocks continue their tech-driven sell-off, prediction markets traders are seeing a bleaker outlook for the remainder of the year.

On platform Kalshi, speculators place just 6% odds that the S&P 500 will cross 8,000 in 2026. On Oct. 13, those odds were as high as 72%.

Contracts ask traders if the S&P 500 will go above various levels in 2026, and they're resolved using data from S&P Dow Jones Indices.

On Friday, the Nasdaq Composite turned negative for the year as investors continued to dump tech stocks, primarily those with exposure to the artificial intelligence build-out. The Dow Jones Industrial Average and the S&P 500 fell into correction territory, too.

The S&P 500 has slashed its year-to-date gain to just over 2%.

The Stock Trader's Almanac in a Thursday note broadly agreed that the hopes for a December rally are fading, with the caveat that a "Santa rally" could still appear at the end of the year.

"The market is under pressure and more downside can't be ruled out," the note said.

The Stock Trader's Almanac sees 2026 following the year-end pattern of 2018, when the Federal Reserve was in a tightening cycle in a midterm year. The Fed is widely expected to hike rates at its December meeting.

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

#2656. Fantasy Basketball Top 200 Rankings: How concerned should Trae Young managers be?

With the Washington Wizards boasting a revamped rotation headlined by first-overall pick AJ Dybantsa and veterans Anthony Davis and Trae Young, there were expectations that the team would take a step forward in 2026-27. While the team's current record of 7-11 doesn't scream out "progress," it is an improvement compared to how the Wizards started last season. But among the concerns has been Young's play, as he is going through one of the most inefficient shooting stretches of his NBA career.

Through 18 games, Young is averaging 17.1 points, 2.8 rebounds, 8.8 assists, 1.3 steals and 2.1 three-pointers per game. The assists, steals and three-pointers have been fine, but the points and percentages have taken significant hits. Young finished Sunday with shooting splits of 36.4 percent from the field and 32.5 percent from beyond the arc. If it held, the former would be the lowest of Young's career by a significant margin, and he hasn't shot this poorly from three since his rookie season (32.4 percent).

Despite Young's continued struggles, Washington went 3-1 last week, including a blowout win over the Magic on November 22. In those four games, Young averaged 12.8 points, 2.3 rebounds, 9.0 assists, 1.8 steals and 2.5 three-pointers per game, shooting 28.6 percent from the field.

He was better from three, making 40 percent of his attempts, and Young has kept his turnover count in check on most nights. Washington's improved rotation should be acknowledged, as it allows Young to take a step back as a scorer. But given his second-round ADP, fantasy managers expected a bigger return than what they've seen so far.

C Alex Sarr, Washington Wizards: Young isn't the only Wizards starter whose production has underwhelmed. Outside of three-pointers, Sarr's averages have decreased across the board compared to his 2025-26 numbers, with the third-year center averaging 11.2 points on eight shot attempts per game.

#2654. Shane Steichen says Colts have not shut down Alec Pierce for the season

A report from NFL Media on Sunday morning indicated that the Colts have decided to pull the plug on hopes that wide receiver Alec Pierce will return to action this season, but it appears to have been premature.

Pierce has not played since aggravating a heel injury that required offseason surgery in Week 2 and the Colts’ recent roster moves have made it clear that they have shifted their attention to the 2027 season. While that may be the case, head coach Shane Steichen said on Monday that the team has not made any call about whether Pierce will return in their final six games.

“I know there’s reports out there on Alec Pierce being done for the season,” Steichen said, via Stephen Holder of ESPN.com. “We have not made that determination. He’s still going through his rehab process. The only way we’ll put him out there is if he’s 100 percent. We gotta be really smart.”

Pierce has a short window to get to 100 percent before the season is over and bringing him back from a long layoff may not be the best way to ensure that he’s at full strength for next season, so it may just be a matter of when rather than if the Colts ultimately decide to shut the wideout down until next year.

#2652. The Nasdaq is in freefall. Here's one stock to look at

I asked Claude after Friday's close what I should do about the tanking stock market. Its advice was surprisingly useful: avoid panic selling and look for opportunities. A 40 VIX, which it touched Friday, might provide those opportunities. Specifically, a higher VIX (higher options premiums) signals fear, but it also creates an opportunity to sell elevated premium in some premium stocks, like Amazon.

Amazon's cloud business is growing at its fastest pace in nearly five years. AWS revenue rose 39.3% YoY in the third quarter, and management reported a $578 billion backlog. Trailing 12-month operating cash flow rose 29% to $168.3 billion. However, infrastructure investment accelerated, resulting in more than $42 billion in negative cash flow. The competing forces of accelerating growth and the cost of supplying it could keep the stock in its range over the next four weeks. Its shares are only off 14% from its recent highs, comparatively better than the Nasdaq Composite's near 19% decline.

AWS demand supports the lower end of the range. The backlog gives Amazon a basis for adding capacity even as investors scrutinize the cash cost. Cash spending could temper an upside rerating.

The next leg higher may require evidence that new capacity can earn attractive returns at this scale, and that evidence may be in short supply over the next couple of months.

Sell the Dec. 18, 2026 AMZN 235/285 strangle for an indicative $5.90 credit per share.

Max reward — $590 per strangle if AMZN finishes between $235 and $285 at expiration, before fees.

Breakevens at expiration — $229.10 and $290.90.

Max risk — Unlimited on the uncovered call, but one should think of the trade as risking getting short at a net price above $290, a nearly 13% premium to today's closing price. The downside loss would reach $22,910 per strangle if AMZN fell to zero. The credit is roughly 2.3% of a $260 reference share price over four weeks, or nearly 30% on a simple annualized basis. That comparison uses the share price as its denominator. Actual return on capital depends on margin and the cost to close or settle the trade.

Buying in bear markets, as the Nasdaq just touched, is never easy. But those opportunities don't present themselves too often. What are you going to do with it?

#2651. Dwyane Wade Backs Davion Mitchell’s Hard Work And Rise

Dwyane Wade is not just watching from the sidelines anymore. The Heat legend is loud and clear about his support for Davion Mitchell. Wade says he is "rocking" with the guard because of the non-stop work Mitchell puts in. This isn't just praise for a good game. It is about the daily grind that most fans never see. The 28-year-old joined the team as part of the Jimmy Butler trade, and his numbers prove he belongs.

At present, Mitchell is averaging 8.6 assists and 17 points. He shoots 51% from the field and 43.3% from deep. These are not small jumps from his past seasons. He went from 9 points a game to 17. That change has helped the offense huge. Per Yahoo, Wade highlighted the effort on his YouTube channel. "A guy that walks around and travel with a basketball everywhere he goes as a grown man, I'm rocking with him," Wade said. He noted that Mitchell always has a ball with him in the summer. That kind of habit shows why he is trusted so much now.

The Heat are on a seven-game winning streak. Mitchell is a big reason for this run. He started alongside Giannis Antetokounmpo, Bam Adebayo, and Klay Thompson. His defense is also top-tier in the league. Before the season, people questioned his playmaking. Now, he has proved them wrong. Wade pointed out how Mitchell ran the offense well against Cleveland. The team jumped from 10th to 4th in the NBA Power Rankings after their recent homestand. According to Yahoo Sports, the Heat now average 126.7 points during this streak. They have the highest-scoring offense in the league right now.

Bam Adebayo also stepped up when Giannis got hurt. Adebayo recorded a triple-double with 24 points, 15 rebounds, and 12 assists. That 12-assist mark is a new career-high for him. "When one of your best players go out the game, the next player has to step up. That was me. So we took care of business," Adebayo said. The team is moving fast and sharing the ball. "We getting stops. We running in transition. We sharing the ball and guys are making plays for one another," he added. This collective effort is what keeps the wins coming.

The winning streak will face a real test soon. The Heat must hit the road for three straight games against Eastern Conference rivals. They play the Philadelphia 76ers on November 25. Then they face the New York Knicks on November 27. The trip ends with a game against the Cleveland Cavaliers on November 29. Adebayo knows the challenge is huge but is ready for it. "It's going to be a gauntlet, but we'll embrace it. We'll try to play collective basketball. Try to keep this winning streak going," Bam said after the game. The Sixers sit at the top of the conference with a 14-3 record. LeBron James driving 16.1 points per game. The Heat will need every bit of their current form to survive this trip.

#2650. Rams TE Terrance Ferguson to undergo season-ending ankle surgery

One of the Rams’ young pass-catchers will not be able to return in 2026.

Via multiple reporters, head coach Sean McVay said on Monday that tight end Terrance Ferguson will undergo season-ending ankle surgery.

Ferguson was on track to return earlier this month before suffering a hamstring injury at practice.

But now his ankle has not responded to treatment the way he or the team anticipated.

“Didn’t necessarily feel great, but he had a really good week of practice but you could still see he was kind of getting comfortable,” McVay said of the process with Ferguson, via Adam Grosbard of the Southern California News Group. “Took some meds to be able to do that. Gets the hamstring on a Friday, and then, as he’s really been attacking both of these injuries, you get another look at it, and it was like something in his deltoid and his ankle that really is just not healing the way that you want.

“I don’t know if it was necessarily a specific setback as much as it’s just not coming along, and he wouldn’t be able to be himself and put his foot in the ground, and a lot of the things that his game is predicated on is being that twitchy athlete, some of the change of direction, moving him across the formation.”

Ferguson is now set to remain on injured reserve for the rest of the year.

A second-round pick in the 2025 draft, Ferguson was able to appear in just three games this year, catching seven passes for 63 yards with a touchdown. He caught 11 passes for 231 yards with three TDs in 14 games as a rookie last year.

#2649. Super Bowl LXI will be shown in IMAX theaters

The Super Bowl will be shown on some very big screens this season.

ESPN’s broadcast of Super Bowl LXI will be shown live in IMAX theaters as part of an agreement among the NFL, Disney and IMAX.

The game will air live in IMAX theaters in the U.S., Australia, New Zealand and Brazil. The show will include the game itself, the halftime show, pregame and postgame.

IMAX has grown increasingly popular for its huge, curved screens and immersive sound. The format is known for movies like The Odyssey, not for live sports, but the opportunity to watch the Super Bowl on the biggest screen in town will draw plenty of fans who will be curious to see what the game looks like on a screen so big that the players are larger than life.

#2646. Cardinals fire defensive coordinator Nick Rallis

Arizona is making a significant change to its coaching staff.

The Cardinals have fired defensive coordinator Nick Rallis, according to NFL Network’s Ian Rapoport.

Rallis, 33, was retained under first-year head coach Mike LaFleur after previously serving in the same role under former head coach Jonathan Gannon. Rallis came with Gannon from Philadelphia, where Gannon had served as defensive coordinator and Rallis was linebackers coach.

The Cardinals have struggled mightily on defense this season, with the unit currently ranked No. 26 in yards allowed and No. 31 in points allowed. The team had offered little resistance in losses to the Rams and Chiefs in consecutive weeks, with both teams scoring 34 points.

Senior defensive assistant Teryl Austin will serve as interim defensive coordinator. He was Pittsburgh’s DC under Mike Tomlin from 2022 to 2025. He previously served as defensive coordinator for the Lions and Bengals.
