#2644. The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats!

The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats! originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

This 76ers team is a heck of a lot of fun to watch and fun basketball means fun stats!

At 13-3, the 76ers are exactly a month into the 5th-best start in franchise history and best since they were 14-2 in 2000-01. Which turned out to be a pretty good year. They were 15-1 in 1949-50 and 1966-67 and 14-2 in 1981-82.

To help everybody get through this interminable two-day gap between 76ers games, here's our first 2026-27 installment of Roob's 76ers Stats, with a tip of the hat to Stathead.

→ The 76ers have been out-rebounded by 34 through 16 games (719-685) and their 13-3 record matches the best in NBA history by a team that's been out-rebounded after 16 games. Three other teams have been 13-3 despite getting out-rebounded, most recently the 2013-14 Miami Heat. The others are the 1979-80 Bucks and 2012-2013 Spurs. The 76ers are an NBA-best 6-3 this year when they've been out-rebounded (which means they're 7-0 when they have a rebounding edge).

→ In the win over Orlando Thursday, V.J. Edgecombe had 24 points, eight rebounds, six assists and two steals, and Joel Embiid had 26 points, six rebounds, seven assists and two steals. It was the first time two 76ers had at least 24 points, six rebounds and assists and two steals in a game in 53 years. In a win over the Celtics at the Spectrum in 1973, Fred Carter had 31 points, six rebounds, six assists and seven steals and George McGinniss had 28 points, 16 rebounds, eight assists and three steals.

→ Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, a career 37 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 44 percent so far. Anfernee Simons, a career 38 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 41 percent. Jabari Walker, a career 33 percent 3-point shooter, is at 42 percent. Dean Wade, a career 37 percent shooter, is at 41 percent. Jaylen Brown, a career 36 percent shooter from range, is at 39 percent. The 76ers are shooting just 36 percent as a team from the stripe because James and Embiid have been so bad, but everybody else is off to career-best starts from 3-point range.

→ The 76ers are 7-1 on the road, equalling their best road record ever after eight games. They were also 7-1 in 1980-81, 1981-82 and 1982-83. The 1981 team had the best road start in franchise history, going 12-1 before losing its second road game.

→ With 15.7 points, 6.8 assists and 6.8 rebounds per game, LeBron James is only the fifth 76er ever to average at least 15 ½ points and 6 ½ assists and rebounds through 16 games. The others are some familiar names: Wilt Chamberlain in 1967, Charles Barkley in 1987, Ben Simmons in 2018 and James Harden in 2023.

→ The 76ers already have more wins this year after trailing by 10 points through the first quarter than all but two full seasons since 1950. They had four in 1985-86 and again in 2022-23. The Hawks also have three wins this year when trailing by double digits after the first quarter, but they're 3-4 and the 76ers are 3-2. So they actually have a winning record when they're down by double digits going into the second quarter.

→ With Jaylen Brown (22.6, 6.6), Joel Embiid (20.0, 8.3), LeBron James (15.7, 6.8) and V.J. Edgecombe (13.6, 6.5), the 76ers are the only NBA team with four players averaging 6 ½ rebounds and 13 points. The last time they had four players in a season who averaged 13 points and 6 ½ rebounds was 1961-62 when they were the Syracuse Nationals, with Dave Gambee (16.7, 7.9), Hal Greer (22.8, 7.4), Red Kerr (16.3, 14.7), Dolph Schayes (14.7, 7.8) and Lee Shaffer (16.9, 6.8).

→ Edgecombe's 6.5 rebounds per game are 2nd-most among shooting guards behind only the Grizzlies' Cedric Coward, who is at 8.5. Edgecombe's 6.5 is also 2nd-highest ever by a 76ers shooting guard behind only Greer's 7.4 during that 1961-62 season. Edgecombe averaged 5.6 rebounds as a rookie and his 5.8 career average is highest by a shooting guard who spent more than one year with the 76ers. Greer is second at 5.3. Among all NBA players who've played at least 90 games, Edgecombe's 5.8 average is 11th-highest all-time among shooting guards (although Stathead includes some swing guard-forwards in their data).

→ Joel Embiid's 14.6 shooting percentage from 3 is worst in NBA history after 16 games (minimum 40 attempts). Embiid is 6-for-41 from the stripe this year. The previous 3-point shooting low 16 games into a season belonged to Cedric Ceballos of the Lakers in 1995-96 and the 76ers' Evan Turner in 2013-14. Both were 6-for-43 for 16.3 percent.

→ LeBron James is at 22.2 percent from 3 on 10-for-45. The Sixers are the first NBA team in 12 years with two players under 23 percent after 16 games with a minimum of 40 attempts. In 2014 Tony Wroten was 10-for-50 (10 percent) and Turner was 6-for-43 (16.3 percent).

→ Baca artikel asli

#2643. Top Wall Street analysts favor these 3 dividend stocks for strong returns

Investors often favor dividend stocks for their regular income and the promise of potential share price appreciation. But selecting the right dividend stocks from a huge pool of companies can be difficult.

Top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend-paying stocks, as their recommendations are backed by thorough analysis of a company's financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Devon Energy (DVN), an independent oil and natural gas exploration and production company, reported better-than-expected third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of 32 cents per share for the fourth quarter, payable December 30. Based on the fixed dividends paid over the trailing 12 months, DVN yields 2.85%.

After hosting a virtual investor meeting with management, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Devon, with a price target of $59. Mehta said that he is now more confident in management's outlook for delivering strong near-term capital returns as well as efforts to drive operational efficiencies and productivity gains.

Devon sees an opportunity to increase share repurchases, driven by solid free cash flow (FCF), contributions from the Eagle Ford divestiture and continued improvement in the balance sheet, Mehta said. Devon expects share buybacks worth more than $1 billion in Q4 2026, up from $414 million in the third quarter, backed by a strong pricing backdrop and improved capital efficiency.

Mehta, a 5-star analyst, said Devon expects about $6.3 billion of excess FCF in 2027, including about $3.5 billion of net proceeds from the Eagle Ford sale. That would enable the company to accelerate buybacks while progressing toward its goal of reducing gross debt to about $9 billion by early 2027. Mehta noted that under DVN's framework to return up to 70% of annual FCF, management favors share buybacks as it believes the stock is discounted compared to large-cap peers.

"Management expects continued capital efficiency gains from merger synergies, extended laterals, surfactant adoption and development of the newly acquired federal acreage," Mehta said.

Mehta ranks No. 876 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.1%. See Devon Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Energy Transfer (ET), is a limited partnership that operates about 140,000 miles of pipeline and associated energy infrastructure. The company recently announced third-quarter results showing net income per common unit growing about 18% year-over-year, to 33 cents. ET's cash distribution for Q3 2026 was 34.25 cents per common unit, up more than 3% year-over-year. At an annualized distribution of $1.37 per share, Energy Transfer yields more than 7%.

TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer with a price target of $25. The company delivered better-than-expected Q3 results and announced the 1.2 billion cubic feet per day (bcf/d) South Mississippi Pipeline, a 215-mile, 36-inch natural gas line linking the Perryville Hub to the Florida Gas Transmission (FGT) network, Gabelman said. The project is supported by 20-year firm transportation agreements and is expected to enter service in 2030. ET expects to ultimately own 51% of the $1.6 billion project.

The 5-star analyst said that Energy Transfer lowered its 2026 capital spending outlook to $5.5 billion (midpoint of the guidance range) from $5.75 billion due to project timing. Energy Transfer expects capex to rise to $7 billion in 2027, while Gabelman projects $5 billion in both 2028 and 2029, based on disclosed projects.

Gabelman also highlighted management's commentary about optimization initiatives contributing an incremental $800 million to 2026 earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization, a larger benefit than in recent years. "A return to less commodity volatility should the [Middle East] war end could bring some relative downside, though this is offset by continued strong growth prospects," he said.

Gabelman ranks No. 397 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.4%. See Energy Transfer KPIs on TipRanks.

Oil and gas exploration and production company ConocoPhillips (COP) delivered better-than-expected Q3 performance. The company announced an 11% hike in its quarterly dividend to 93 cents per share, effective with the Q4 dividend payable on December 1. Based on the dividends paid over the trailing 12 months, COP yields about 2.6%.

Following the Q3 results, Wells Fargo analyst Sam Margolin repeated a buy rating on ConocoPhillips, citing strong potential for further dividend increases. Margolin also trimmed his price target to $185 from $189 to reflect timing effects and the WAHA forward curve, though he said that COP could still re-rate as its natural portfolio lifecycle provides stronger protection against inflationary pressures than peers. The Waha forward curve tracks future natural gas prices based on the West Texas Waha hub.

Margolin, another 5-star analyst, highlighted that ConocoPhillips' Q3 2026 adjusted earnings per share of $3.27 was well above Wells Fargo and the Street's estimates of $3.04 and $3.05, respectively. Earnings were driven by solid price realizations, partially offset by higher depletion, depreciation and amortization and purchased commodities.

Margolin also noted COP's dividend increase and said the accelerated buyback enabled by current commodity prices could further support long-term dividend growth. He sees the possibility of ConocoPhillips' dividend growth rate exceeding its current 6% cost of equity into the end of the decade. Margolin expects COP to sustain an 8% dividend compound annual growth rate through 2029 while repurchasing $6.4 billion of stock annually, and reducing net debt-to-capital to less than 9% by the end of 2028 based on the assumption of an average Brent crude oil price of $75 a barrel.

"The message on the 3Q call was similar to that of 2Q, reiterating the moderation in reinvestment rate and commitment to dividend growth through the Willow start-up and after," Margolin said.

Margolin ranks No. 189 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 69% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.6%. See ConocoPhillips Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2642. Mike LaFleur: Defense needed a fresh voice

Cardinals head coach Mike LaFleur made a significant change to his coaching staff on Monday.

Defensive coordinator Nick Rallis was dismissed a day after the Cardinals gave up 435 yards in a 34-7 loss to the Chiefs. They lost 34-6 to the Rams the prior week and LaFleur said the team is trying to find solutions for a unit that hasn't "stopped the bleeding enough.

"I just felt in my heart this morning we needed a change," LaFleur said, via the team's website. "A fresh voice."

LaFleur acknowledged that an offense that's scored 16 points while starting three different quarterbacks in the last three weeks hasn't "held our end of the bargain," but the team has not made any coaching changes on that side of the ball

Senior defensive assistant Teryl Austin will take on the defensive coordinator role on an interim basis. Lafleur said he will "put his own spin" on how the team plays on that side of the ball over the final six games.

#2641. Vikings "don't necessarily expect" Jordan Addison back this week

The Vikings didn’t have wide receiver Jordan Addison in the lineup for Sunday night’s game against the 49ers and his availability for Week 12 came up during head coach Kevin O’Connell’s Monday press conference.

#2640. Fantasy Basketball Week 5 High Score Perfect Lineup: 3 players from Bulls-Nuggets OT track meet make the list

It's Thanksgiving week in the NBA and looking back, fantasy basketball managers have plenty to be thankful for (if they were able to avoid the injury bug after the first month-plus of the 2026-27 season).

In Yahoo High Score fantasy basketball, it was a pretty eventful Week 5 with a few 50-burgers and three players landing in the perfect lineup thanks to an overtime track meet between the Bulls and Nuggets on Sunday. Let's dive into the perfect lineup and break down the top performers.

Josh Giddey: The Bulls guard had been off to a slow start to the season, averaging just 8.5 points and 7.5 assists per game in six contests in the opening month. But since November came along, Giddey has upped his game a bit, though managers would like to see him be more aggressive on offense. But that unselfishness is why Giddey landed in the perfect lineup, posting 23 points, 14 rebounds and 22 assists for 84 fantasy points in the Bulls' OT loss to Denver on Sunday. Chicago has three gettable matchups in Week 6, so be sure to get Giddey in those lineups.

Jamal Murray: The Nuggets' guard is pacing toward his best NBA season, averaging 26.2 points per game after Sunday's 45-point outburst in the win over Chicago. That point-per-minute performance awarded Murray 79 fantasy points and one of the guard spots in the perfect lineup. Murray also chipped in 5 rebounds, 7 assists and 5 stocks — pretty good category coverage. A consensus second-round pick preseason, Murray is looking like an early league-winner if he can keep this up.

Victor Wembanyama: Wow, we didn't expect Wemby to land in this article again (wink). But really, Wembanyama is putting together an MVP campaign so far in 2026-27, and has been arguably the best asset for High Score fantasy basketball (Luka Dončić is really the only one ahead of him right now). After last week's 50-point outing against the Pelicans, Wembanyama now has the two highest single outputs of the season for High Score (110, 97). He'll get another shot to try and top the High Score leaderboard in Week 6 with four games on tap.

Nikola Jokić: The Joker benefitted from that overtime contest on Sunday, same as Giddey and Murray. It was the Nuggets big's second-highest fantasy score of the season with a 37-18-11 triple-double, just his fourth triple-double of the season. Yes, we can say "just" there because we're used to Jokić doing that pretty much every night. While this feels like a slow start by Jokić's standards, he's still the No. 3 asset in fantasy as we're just past the first month of the season.

Bam Adebayo: The Heat and Adebayo started off the Giannis era with four straight losses but have since gone 10-2 since Oct. 28. Adebayo is starting to find his groove on offense, averaging north of 20 points per game in November with 8.4 rebounds, 3.9 assits and nearly 3.0 stocks per contest. Other than a game in which he left early due to injury, Adebayo has at least 40 fantasy points in six straight games. Miami has a rough sledding up in Week 6 with road games against Eastern Conference rivals — the Sixers, Knicks and Cavaliers.

Tyler Herro: The former Heat guard who was the centerpiece of the Giannis deal this offseason, Herro was expected to join Milwaukee and lead the team in scoring. That's been the case, though it hasn't led to much winning for the Bucks. That doesn't matter to us in fantasy, just as long as Herro is filling up the score sheet. Last Saturday, Herro scored a career-high 50 points with 11 boards and 7 assists in a close loss to Philadelphia. Milwaukee has a four-game week with some spots where Herro can showcase that scoring touch again.

#2638. Cignetti non-committal on staring QB against Purdue, but No. 12 Indiana could be missing its center

BLOOMINGTON, Ind. (AP) — Indiana coach Curt Cignetti remains hopeful some of his injured players will return for Saturday's regular-season finale against rival Purdue, but is less optimistic about starting center Bray Lynch — and won't say whether Josh Hoover or Grant Wilson will be the starting quarterback.

Cignetti made the comments during his weekly news conference Monday.

Wilson relieved a banged-up Hoover in the second half against Minnesota on Oct. 31 and rallied Indiana to a win. Hoover injured his right shoulder against then No. 2 Ohio State, finished the game and then took another big hit against Minnesota before he was replaced.

The next week, Wilson started and led the Hoosiers past then No. 15 USC for their only win over a ranked opponent this season. Wilson started last weekend at Washington before Hoover relieved him in the second half.

Who starts this weekend when No. 12 Indiana meets rival Purdue (3-8, 2-6 Big Ten ) is unclear.

"I think it's been pretty easy to gauge, all you've got to do is look at the ball velocity and how far he can throw it," Cignetti said when asked about Hoover. "I thought he came in and gave us a spark, but still some (missed opportunities) there at the end, plays we've got to make. Both quarterbacks will take reps with the ones (in practice), and we'll evaluate it as the week goes on."

Last weekend's 27-24 overtime loss at Washington put the Hoosiers' (9-2, 6-2, No. 8 CFP) hopes of defending their Big Ten and national championships in peril, and now they may be even more short-handed.

Lynch was one of two offensive linemen who left the game. He had lower leg injury. Adedamola Ajani also left but returned after missing two plays. Another starter, Joe Brunner, suffered a season-ending knee injury earlier this month in practice. As usual, Cignetti didn't divulge many specifics.

"The only thing I will say about the injuries is we expect to get some guys back, and the only one that doesn't look good right now is Bray Lynch," he said.

Cignetti acknowledged the depth issues along the offensive line forced the Hoosier to make some adjustments to the game plan.

Indiana also has been without key linebacker Rolijah Hardy for the past three games. He hurt his back in warmups before the overtime win over Minnesota on Oct. 31.

Cornerback A.J. Harris missed yet another game with a leg injury Saturday, and he was joined on the sideline by defensive lineman Kellen Wyatt and starting running back Turbo Richard, who has missed two straight games with a shoulder injury.

Another starter, receiver Nick Marsh, left the Washington game with an undisclosed injury late in regulation. He did not return, and Marsh used social media to send thanks for the support he had been receiving from fans over the weekend.

#2637. The biggest money-making energy trade looks over. What to buy instead

Is one of the greatest money-making trades of the last few years coming to an end?

No, I'm not talking about Nvidia or any of the data center- or AI-related power companies (although they have been crushed lately too… more on that on CNBC TV. Tune in!).

I'm talking about whether the epic boom in refinery-related stocks is unwinding. Over the last year, the jump in some of these companies has been nothing short of remarkable.

That jump is largely due to massive increases in margins, earnings, and cash flow. Those have occurred because of a big jump in what is called a crack spread. That is the difference between the price of oil and the finished price of refined products such as diesel and jet fuel. For most of this year, spreads have been soaring as disruptions and attacks on facilities and shipping in the Arabian Gulf, Hormuz, and deep inside Russia have played havoc with the market. In the last week or so, however, something big has changed: many of those spreads have started to tighten.

This seismic drop in spreads is causing a selloff in the aforementioned refinery companies.

If spreads continue to fall, these stocks may well keep selling off from recent highs. The upshot for investors may be that, as the shares come down, analysts see more upside. The median price target for Phillips 66 (PSX) now indicates a 20% gain from here, while smaller-cap Par Pacific (PARR) is double that.

Here are a few other key points to focus on heading into the Thanksgiving-shortened week.

I like to keep politics as much at arm's length as possible — we all know that energy and politics (sadly) often go hand in hand. Energy policies coming from Washington, D.C., can drive — or destroy — investment dollars. With all the big shifts in the White House over the last 10 years (four years of Trump, four years of Biden, and now 2 years into Trump #2), energy policies can blow through Washington faster than a summer squall hits a wind turbine. But because federal policies can mean so much for specific companies, sectors, and investments, it's important to follow the news flow.

That said, I'd like to take a moment to introduce you to the Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act, known as BAAJA (and pronounced like the state in Mexico). Like much legislation, BAAJA is an obscure but important bill working its way through the American Congress. BAAJA does a lot of things, but what Power Insider readers care about is its focus on energy permitting reform. In a nutshell, BAAJA would help speed up the building of new electricity infrastructure. Again, that's an overly simplistic view of a big bill, but it needs to be on your radar. The utility trade group Edison Electric Institute came out in favor of the legislation this month after the industry received some 11th-hour changes. See more about it in our recent interview with EEI President Drew Maloney here.

The bill passed the U.S. Senate in a vote late Thursday night. Now it moves to the House. Support for its final passage is also coming from the big LNG trade group, whose executive director said he "applauds the Senate for its bipartisan effort to pass meaningful, bipartisan, and durable permitting reforms aimed at delivering greater predictability, consistency, and certainty in the permitting, production, and export process for U.S. LNG. This legislation moves us closer to untangling red tape and eliminating overlapping agency reviews that can keep U.S. energy projects delayed for years."

The Natural Gas Supply Association also chimed in, saying it urges Congress to see the bill as "a resource-agnostic and balanced reform agenda for boosting American energy security. It keeps strong environmental reviews in place, but it draws a firm line against the endless procedural delays that tie critical energy projects in knots for a decade."

In my view, passing BAAJA is an important test of Congress's appetite for helping the energy and electrical power industry, lowering costs, and speeding time to market. The old adage was correct: time is money. Literally. If BAAJA moves on through the House, we could see an upside move in some utility stocks and any company related to energy infrastructure.

Speaking of politics, the news is getting lost in the massive Anthropic (ANT) IPO, but the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality (TCEQ) — whose importance I wrote about in the last edition — just decided to resume permitting for data centers. The Texas Tribune did a nice write-up on it, and you can read it here.

It's tough to say, but it's true. The two are inextricably linked by policies created at both the federal and state levels. As I wrote in the last edition, some federally elected officials may have less impact on state-level issues — like the data center buildout — but they certainly have power over national laws. While many of you are enthralled, disgusted, or confused by sordid tales of Nvidia and Jensen-gate, keep one eye open on what happens in Washington, D.C.

Have a great week, and have a wonderful Thanksgiving, everyone.

Perhaps the big investor story — which is, like most things lately, related to Jensen-gate and Nvidia—is the sizable selloff in many AI, neocloud, and data center power-related stocks.

Painful for current shareholders, but big drops can also bring big opportunities. Wall Street believes that's the case, at least right now. Look at some of the potential upside based on the stocks' prices versus their price targets.

We spoke with the CEO of Applied Digital (APLD) about all of this on CNBC last week, and you can watch it here. He highlighted how the market may be getting these stock stories wrong because, at least with his company, he has long-term contracts with solid-credit companies. In that way, Applied Digital is more like a landlord than a "pure-play" AI company.

My take → While I'm not going to recommend a specific stock, I would add that it is critical for investors to understand the differences between these companies. While they may have some things in common, they are also different, with different business models, customers, leases, and sources of revenue.

Speaking of recommendations… here are two ideas for you:

A recent decline presents a nice opportunity in SOLV Energy (MWH). UBS analyst Jon Windham upgrades SOLV to buy from neutral, believing the market is "underappreciating several key tailwinds for SOLV given uncertainty around data center demand and higher interest rates." UBS highlights how the stock has fallen lately despite making two deals and beating and raising recent guidance. UBS says now is an "attractive entry point" for SOLV. (Side note: MWH is a great stock ticker.)

While markets overall fall, BMO remains bullish on Bloom Energy (BE). After speaking with the company, the analyst is upgrading Bloom to outperform, saying he remains "increasingly confident of BE's commercial traction" and that he still has conviction even with the recent drop in shares. While analyst Ameet Thakkar did cut his target to $230 from $260, that's still 50% upside from the current price. Bloom shares have tumbled 45% since October 1.

I was recently asked for my opinion on the price of oil. Here's my take. The market clearly wants to push oil contract prices lower and has for a few months. But, as I've said and reported for a while, don't confuse the futures (paper) price with the actual price. Yes, shipping rates and insurance rates have come down from recent highs — and there is a good amount of oil now flowing through Hormuz — but the cost of a physical barrel of crude is still well above the futures contract. If we get real, longer-lasting peace around Iran, oil prices will fall. There is plenty of oil in the world. That said, if bombs and missiles start firing again, oil prices will firm. I know that's a non-answer answer, but the oil market is truly headline-driven right now. My late good friend Boone Pickens (later in life, he didn't like being called by the "T" in his first name) used to avoid giving a set price target and instead give a directional quote, so I'll do the same: the odds of oil being lower in two months than it is today are 75%.

Last week, I had a one-on-one with Vimal Kapur, chair and CEO of Honeywell International (HON), at an RBC conference in New York City. While the conversation wasn't filmed, I can tell you that Kapur offered investors reassurance that his order book is doing very, very well. This is important because Honeywell is huge in the things that make other things work. Its sensors go into a ton of energy facilities, from refineries to LNG facilities. Honeywell is a leading indicator of spending and buildouts, so if it keeps getting orders, it's a good sign for macro energy.

Bottom line: Watch Honeywell stock as a potential "tell."

This week's Inside Line interview takes a different approach. We hit our friend Craig Johnson up for actionable insights into energy and stocks. Craig is managing director and chief market technician at Piper Sandler. He recently appeared on Power Lunch to talk about markets and stocks. He mentioned being bullish on energy, so we wanted to dig in just a little bit more.

This week, we had the pleasure of chatting with Balaji Krishnamurthy, president of Chevron Australia. We spoke at length about natural gas, the impact of disruptions, and long-term demand from Chevron customers in the Asia-Pacific region.

This is a bit of a local RBI for the New York City area, but one I felt was important to highlight. New York's electric grid operator just came out with a massive warning for everyone in our region: in just a couple of years, there may not be enough electricity supply to power our needs. A new report shows a deficit in seven years, with a nearly 2 GW power hole in a decade. That's a number of big power plants' worth of energy. Get your solar panels on and buy that backup generator! I'll call this one random, but scary.

Catch up with more on energy including interviews and video content from CNBC and Power Insider.

Read the last issue of Power Insider here.

#2635. NBA Game Preview: Timberwolves vs Blazers Thread

Julius Randle returns to the Target Center in his first game against his former team. The storyline of giddiness vs moodiness has been writ large over this whole season and Randle has been pointed to as the source of the latter. This is one of two games that the Nets will be showcased on national TV as they expect a Randle revenge performance.

Given how Brooklyn has rallied around Randle, which is to say, they haven't, this is a game the Wolves should demonstrate the power of positive thinking, or at least consistent effort.

Who: Minnesota Timberwolves (12-5) vs. Brooklyn Nets (3-12)

When: Monday, November 23rd at 6:30 PM CT

Some of the stats above are the usual suspects when comparing teams. Others are a bit cherry picked for the sake of comparing these teams. The Nets are bad. They had five draft picks last year and only three of them are getting playtime.

They are young or disinterested in playing defense. Noah Clowney is the one of few players bringing energy but that can quickly bubble over into earning a technical. Conspiratorial podcaster Michael Porter Jr. is close to, if not their best player and he has never been known to be much of a deterrent. We know that Julius Randle, when dialed in, can be a bulldog on defense. So far this season he has been mostly checked out and only looking for his shot.

The Wolves offense, while backsliding in rank over the past few games, should make mincemeat of this Nets squad. I half-expect the Nets to put Porter Jr. on Jaden McDaniels. MPJ has all the physical tools to hound McDaniels and none of the want-to. The rest of the Wolves should be looking to feed Jaden when he cuts, which should be often.

The Nets do offer a bit of size. Their frontcourt starters of Randle, Porter Jr. Day'Ron Sharpe, and Danny Wolf are all 6'9" and bigger, not to mention they bring one of my least favorite players Mo Wagner off the bench (if Mo so much as lays a finger on Joan Beringer, I riot.) And yet, Brooklyn are bottom in the league at rebounding. This is a team loaded with talent and little want to. Nets head coach Jordi Fernández has his work cut out for him helping the team's youthful rebuild while managing his vets bubbling tempers or conspiracy theories.

The Wolves, who traded away the "size" of Randle and Naz Reid, have been a top rebounding team this season mostly for the fact that when the ball is loose, they are scrambling for it.

To tie this into one of my favorite quotes from Minnesota Lynx head coach Cheryl Reeve on KFAN a number of years ago:

2. Defensive Rebounding: If you are willing to put in the work to make the opponent miss, then finish the play.

In his two years in Minnesota, Julius Randle was instrumental in making deep playoff runs. His game was not pretty. Some offensive possessions he seemed to spend whole shot clock drilling for oil under Target Center, using the ball as his shovel. He was able to use his size to wear down defenders before barreling to the rim, pulling away for a mid-range two, or dishing out to a cutting shooter beyond the arc.

As a point-forward, he offered the Wolves a much needed dimension when Anthony Edwards was out. There are few defenders in the league that can stifle Randle one-on-one on the block. He rarely shrank from the moment, though on several occasions, he missed the moment all together.

His defense would morph from bodying up his man to full matador, sometimes in the same game. If his matchup could hit a three-point shot (thinking about some of those Jaren Jackson Jr. games) his closeouts were always lackluster. Those burly shoulders would shrink when he didn't get the ball and he'd jog back to the other side of the court on a fastbreak. His replacement, Jonathan Kuminga, has been the polar opposite.

Some of that is due to role. Randle was asked to be the engine of an offense. JK has been asked to be the oil that allows the engine to run smoothly. Still, Kuminga has offered up defensive resistance against the best forwards in the game no matter where the attack is coming from. He gets his shots in the flow of the offense and sprints back to defend regardless if he touched the ball or not. What it really comes down to is consistency and that is where Randle's struggles truly lay.

He is an All-NBA talent on any night of the week, just not every night of the week.

Fingers crossed that the Wolves do not come out overconfident like their game against the Bulls. The Randle element should likely add some fire to this matchup and if the Wolves start hot, they should run laps around the Nets. Randle will likely have some move against Rudy or a full shot clock burning possession before he spins for a pull-up jumper. He will jaw at the bench, and the Wolves lead will be shrunk to 24. Maybe Ben Saraf and Drake Powell will get some burn by the end.

Our blogging buddies are over at Nets Daily, make sure to interact with them with courtesy.

#2634. Nvidia surge: What investors should do with the stock and the AI trade now

Nvidia shares gapped higher at the open by more than 30%, on pace for its biggest one-day gain in 23 years on Monday, following the news just before the bell that former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is going to be reinstated.

Investors and traders said that while this reflex rally may have more to run, cautiousness is warranted on Nvidia and the AI trade because of the overhang of investigations into the alleged Chinese espionage involving the company and others, as well as the possibility for data center moratoriums as Democrats take control of Congress and key governor posts in January.

"Am I as bullish on Nvidia now as I was on Friday? Of course not, you can't be," said Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, which counted Nvidia as its largest position as he bought on the dip over the last few weeks. "I'm trimming back."

"All the same issues that were here before Jensen resigned are still here," he added.

The day before Huang resigned on Nov. 12 as CEO, Nvidia closed at $173.73. It's intraday high on Monday was $171.50, up from its recent low around $125 as it was selling off in the wake controversies. The VanEck Semiconductor ETF closed at $506.96 the day before his resignation. It reached a high of $469.35 before losing steam as trading wore on Monday.

Mike Khouw, YieldMax strategist at Tidal Financial Group, told CNBC on Monday that there were "sighs of relief," "applause" and even "cheering" among investors at the news of Huang's potential reinstatement. But he also said there are still considerable risks facing the AI trade.

Jensen Huang was not the only problem at Nvidia, he said, and there are still many vulnerabilities in the overall AI trade, which should give investors pause. Credit-sensitive companies are still at risk of faltering, Khouw added.

The national security implications of the scandal that has rocked Nvidia and the global AI trade more broadly aren't going away any time soon even as the Trump administration assured the Nvidia board there were none. Investors on Wall Street are expressing strategic concerns about ways to play the tech space just as lawmakers in Washington are demanding answers.

Democrats are demanding answers on what exactly happened with Nvidia and the Chinese government.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-Mass.) said in a Monday statement. "The Trump Administration also needs to explain to Congress what 'input' was given and by which senior officials in the United States government to support his reinstatement."

House Ways and Means ranking member Richard Neal (D-Mass.) stressed the importance of Congressional oversight in determining the best course of action to move forward. The outcome of any Congressional investigations will likely be crucial for Nvidia's long-term viability as an investment opportunity, especially since the company does so much international business.

"This is an export control issue," Neal told CNBC on Monday. "Congress is not subservient to this president or any other president as it relates to trade and tariff policies. This is a trade related issue."

Many of the same Democrats will call for a slowdown in the data center buildout, a strong issue for the public partly responsible for their Blue Wave earlier this month.

"You're going to have to work your way through what these data center moratoriums all mean," said Niles. "Now I think the broader market keeps going but I am not as convinced on the move in the data-center centric names from here."

To be sure, many Wall Street analysts were rushing to upgrade Nvidia's stock on Monday after Huang's reinstatement. Most restored price targets well above $200 with one as high as $270. But many traders think the rebound may fail before getting back to those levels.

Famed short seller Michael Burry, who made a boatload betting against Nvidia with put options as it cratered, told his Substack audience that he thinks the rebound here could be "outsized," especially with low volume in the market overall because of the Thanksgiving holiday this week.

But he too sees the rally eventually losing steam.

"Jensen being back without much more information might even restore NVDA to the high 100s," Burry wrote. "I won't short it on this information I have today until it gets closer to or maybe hurdles $200."

#2633. ESPN bringing Super Bowl LXI to IMAX theaters

This February, you'll be able to watch the Big Game on the Big Screen.

Disney announced on Monday that its broadcast of Super Bowl LXI will be available live in IMAX theaters across the United States and "select international territories," according to Variety.

The IMAX experience will feature ESPN's primary telecast (which, at least for now, will be called by Joe Buck and Troy Aikman), the halftime show headlined by Jay-Z, as well as pregame and postgame studio coverage. Moviegoers will start seeing promotional spots for the four-hour presentation this week. Ticketing information will be announced at a later date.

The news gives Super Bowl viewers yet another way to consume the game. ESPN and Disney have already announced a variety of alternate telecasts for the game, which is the first Super Bowl to ever air on ESPN airwaves. ESPN2 will feature an altcast with Peyton and Eli Manning, ESPNU will feature Pat McAfee and friends, NFL Network will produce an analytics-focused presentation, ESPN Deportes will carry a Spanish-language broadcast, and Disney Channel will have a Star Wars-themed animated altcast.

Movie theaters have collaborated to showcase major live sporting events in the past, including IMAX's recent presentation of the Formula One World Championship.

"The Super Bowl is a one-of-a-kind global cultural event imbued with spectacle, sound, drama, and heroes — all the makings of a great IMAX experience," IMAX CEO Rich Gelfond said, per Variety. "We're excited to work in partnership with Disney, ESPN and the NFL to give football fans an unforgettable night."

#2631. Nets gets crushing injury update on Nolan Traore — with Mikel Brown set for big chance

MINNEAPOLIS — Brooklyn's Nolan Traore will miss at least the next two months with a hamstring injury, in a tough break for both the fast-rising Frenchman and his struggling Nets.

Traore was diagnosed with a right hamstring strain, and it will be eight-to-10 weeks before he's even re-evaluated, according to the team.

The young point guard suffered the injury in the third quarter of Friday night's 113-111 loss to the visiting Orlando Magic. Traore limped off the Barclays Center floor under his own power and didn't return. With this news, he's not expected back until the calendar flips to 2027.

It's unclear what Brooklyn's plans are at the point guard position, though this does open the door wide for rookie Mikel Brown Jr. to make his first start. The lottery pick has been on a minutes limit and treated with kid gloves to this point.

A trainer checks on Brooklyn Nets guard Nolan Traore (10) during the second half against the Orlando Magic in Brooklyn, N.Y., Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

The Nets play Monday night at the Minnesota Timberwolves, and coach Jordi Fernandez's decision will speak volumes.

Off guard Egor Demin is another option, with the 2025 lottery pick being the only other Net who has started at the lead guard spot this season.

After Demin had been starting at point guard during his rookie campaign — and sorely miscast — Traore supplanted him midway through the season. Now the young Frenchman had taken a quantum leap forward this year, and improved the most of the so-called Flatbush Five — Brooklyn's quintet of 2025 first-round picks.

Traore went from averaging 8.9 points on .380 shooting and .318 from 3-point range as a rookie to his current 12.9 points on .511 overall and .404 from deep.

The game had clearly slowed down for the speedy Frenchman, and he was learning how to not only use his quickness but his newfound strength and size to not get bodied off the ball.

As a rookie, Traore's finishing at the rim had ranked dead last in the NBA among players with at least 130 attempts, at just 44.6 percent according to Synergy. But after a summer spent bulking up, he'd raised that to 56.4%. He'd also worked on a repeatable shooting form, and improved on catch-and-shoot 3s.

Traore had averaged 15.5 points on white-hot .537 shooting and .420 from behind the arc in his last 11 games before getting hurt.

That injury does create opportunity for Brown.

The sixth overall pick — the highest selection for the Nets in 16 years — still has not started a game for Brooklyn. Fernandez has pointed to Brown's minutes limit, but the young guard has logged 27:43 in an overtime loss at Boston, 27:23 last Wednesday against Chicago and 26:56 in the Garden against the Knicks.

Demin has gotten the nod at point guard four times this season – including in a jumbo lineup with everybody 6-foot-9 or taller, the first in the league since April 21, 1990. But the Russian is best suited as an off-ball gravity player, and Fernandez has even admitted as much.

Brooklyn Nets' Mikel Brown Jr. dribbles down court against the New York Knicks at Madison Square Garden in New York, Monday, Nov. 2, 2026. Jason Szenes for the New York Post

Danny Wolf and Terance Mann will likely be used to at least help with secondary ballhandling duties — especially when Demin is at the point — and 20-year-old Ben Saraf could be called up from the G League. The Israeli — who is the Nets' second-youngest player behind Traore — is a pure point guard and has been playing well for Long Island, averaging 16.4 points and 8.6 assists.

#2629. NFL on Thanksgiving full schedule, more to know about holiday games

Before you scarf down the turkey, gobble up some NFL football on Thanksgiving.

The 2026 Thanksgiving schedule features some past and some present of the NFL. As usual, the Dallas Cowboys and Detroit Lions will take the field, continuing one of the league's traditions on Turkey Day.

Capping off the day, one of the NFL's most exciting present-day rivalries takes center stage, as the Kansas City Chiefs (8-2) travel to Orchard Park, New York to take on the Buffalo Bills (9-1) in what could be an AFC championship game preview.

Here's how the games line up on Thanksgiving:

Detroit Lions players celebrate their 23-20 win against the Chicago Bears with a turkey drumstick at Ford Field on Nov. 28, 2024. The celebration was made possible by some highly questionable clock management by Bears head coach Matt Eberflus. A day later, Eberflus was fired by the Bears, the first in-season firing of a head coach in franchise history.

Dallas Cowboys running back Ezekiel Elliott poses with the Galloping Gobbler trophy after the win over Washington at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas on Nov 24, 2016. The Cowboys won the game, 31-26. Dak Prescott and Ezekiel Elliott put on an offensive showcase.

During a Thanksgiving Day game in 2013, Pittsburgh Steelers head coach Mike Tomlin got into the action. The Baltimore Ravens' Jacoby Jones broke free on a kickoff return, but Tomlin stepped onto the field, causing Jones to avoid the Steelers coach along the sideline. Instead of a touchdown, Jones was tackled after a 73-yard return. The Ravens went on to win the game, 22-20.

New York Jets quarterback Mark Sanchez collides with the backside of lineman Brandon Moore and fumbles the ball. It was recovered and returned for a touchdown by the New England Patriots' Steve Gregory. The play was part of a disastrous sequence for the Jets, who lost three fumbles as the Patriots scored three touchdowns in 52 seconds. New England went on to win 49-19, and Sanchez's infamous moment from the 2012 game is known as "The Butt Fumble."

Dallas Cowboys quarterback Tony Romo celebrates a touchdown against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers in the third quarter NFL action in Irving, Texas Nov. 23, 2006. In a breakout game, Romo threw for 306 yards and five TDs.

On his way to 49 touchdown passes for the season, Peyton Manning put six on the board in the Indianapolis Colts' masterful 41-9 win over the Detroit Lions. Manning went 23-of-28 for 236 yards and six touchdowns before being pulled near the end of third quarter in 2004.

Randy Moss added another tally to his incredible rookie year in in the Minnesota Vikings' 46-36 win over the Dallas Cowboys, catching three touchdown passes for 163 yards in 1998.

With the Detroit Lions and Pittsburgh Steelers headed to overtime, referee Phil Luckett's microphone caught Pittsburgh captain Jerome Bettis calling "tails" on the coin toss. Despite the toss coming up tails, Luckett claimed Detroit won the toss. The Lions opted to receive the kickoff and scored a field goal on the first possession to win the game in 1998.

Hall of Famer Barry Sanders ran for 167 yards and three touchdowns as the Detroit Lions obliterated the Chicago Bears, 55-20, in 1997. Sanders moved into second place on the NFL’s all-time rushing yardage list during the game.

#2623. Florida State hiring Georgia DC Glenn Schumann as next football coach

Florida State is poised to begin a new era of Seminoles football, with the school prepared to formally name Georgia defensive coordinator Glenn Schumann as its next head coach, USA TODAY Sports has learned from people directly involved in the process.

Considered one of college football's top coordinators on either side of the ball, the 36-year-old Schumann will be a first-time head coach when he takes over the Seminoles. The two sides met in person on Sunday to finalize an agreement.

Schumann built his reputation as a top defensive assistant, first at Alabama under Nick Saban and then at Georgia under Kirby Smart.

College football hot seat: Heat turning up on Bill Belichick, Deion Sanders

A native of Valdosta, Georgia, less than 80 miles from FSU's Tallahassee campus, Schumann also has a connection to new Florida State athletic director Jeff Purinton. The two worked together during Schumann's nearly decade-long tenure at Alabama.

Individuals with knowledge of the search, who spoke to USA TODAY Sports on condition of anonymity to discuss sensitive details, said FSU's promised commitment to funding both the roster and a competitive salary pool for assistant coaches and support staff was instrumental in landing Schumann.

Schumann replaces Mike Norvell, who was fired Sunday, Nov. 1, despite FSU owing him a $50 million buyout. Norvell peaked with the Seminoles during the 2022 and 2023 seasons, when the program won a combined 23 games.

But Norvell never led Florida State to the College Football Playoff. The Seminoles' last appearance came in the inaugural CFP in 2014 under then-coach Jimbo Fisher.

With Florida State mired in a 10-22 stretch since the start of the 2024 season, Norvell was dismissed.

Now, Schumann has been tapped to lead the rebuild.

#2622. Cardinals fire defensive coordinator Nick Rallis after blowout loss to Chiefs: 'We needed a change, a fresh voice'

The Arizona Cardinals fired defensive coordinator Nick Rallis on Monday, a day after a 34-7 loss to the Kansas City Chiefs.

Head coach Mike LaFleur confirmed Rallis' dismissal in his weekly news conference. He said the decision to fire Rallis was made out of a need for a "fresh perspective."

"Nick's a guy that I have a lot of respect for as a person, as a coach," LaFleur said. "… I just felt in my heart this morning that we needed a change. We needed a fresh perspective, a fresh voice."

Senior defensive assistant Teryl Austin will take over as interim defensive coordinator. Austin has previously worked as the defensive coordinator for the Lions, Bengals and Steelers, most recently with Pittsburgh from 2022-25.

Sunday's loss dropped the Cardinals to 3-8. They've allowed 30-plus points in eight of their 11 games and rank 31st in the NFL with 29.4 points allowed per game. They rank 26th with 355.2 yards allowed per game.

The Cardinals have plenty of issues beyond their defense. They rank 25th in total offense (290 ypg) and 29th in scoring (17.4 ppg) as the owners of the worst record in the NFC.

Former Broncos head coach Nathaniel Hackett is Arizona's offensive coordinator. Hackett has had four previous stops as an NFL offensive coordinator, including for LaFleur's brother, Matt LaFleur, with the Green Bay Packers. Mike LaFleur acknowledged Monday that the Cardinals need to improve across the board.

"It's not been good enough collectively, all phases as a team. I'm in the business, and it's my job to find solutions, and this is one that we're trying to find. …

"We're all in this thing together. If you're a real man in this league, you're gonna look at yourself in the mirror first."

#2618. Week in Review: Signature win remains elusive, but that could change soon

Welcome to Week in Review: a Monday feature that looks back at the week that was for the San Antonio Spurs, takes a look at the week ahead, and more. Enjoy!

Week 4: Another undefeated week stretched the Spurs' winning streak out to 12, with the young core spreading the wealth. Victor Wembanyama put on a show for his second Western Conference Player of the Week award this season, beginning with finishing off their road trip with a blowout win in Phoenix and come-from-behind win against the Bulls. They then briefly returned home for an absolute beatdown of the Steph Curry-less Warriors, led by a season-high performance from Stephon Castle to move to the top of their Cup group, followed by a career-high 31 points from Dylan Harper to beat the Jazz and complete the season sweep (yes, already).

The Spurs seemed overdue for a challenge, and they finally got one in a Pistons squad that was shorthanded but still featured Cade Cunningham, who has been making his own early case for MVP. After getting out to an early 14-point lead, the Spurs offense fell off as the Pistons found ways to keep Wemby out of the paint, and no one else could get anything going. They also greatly suffered in the non-Wemby minutes, and Detroit rallied for the win. However, the biggest worry was Harper having to leave the game late after an awkward fall. While he is expected to miss 2-3 weeks with a hip strain, it could have been much worse had it been the groin, so they got lucky there.

It wasn't impressive, but the Spurs found a way to get back on track. Overcoming a season-high 21 turnovers and hot three-point shooting from the Clippers, they used strong defense and timely shot-making in the fourth quarter to pull away. Castle put up a classic "PG Castle" stat line of 21 efficient points, 12 assists, 8 turnovers and 4 steals (it was very much a case of taking the good with the bad), and the bench unit had one of its better outings of the season, with everyone posting a positive plus/minus, including Luke Kornet!

With Zion Williamson a late scratch, depleting the Pelicans even further, Jeremiah Fears showing no fear (get it?) by getting chippy with the Spurs and baiting them into a couple of technicals in an up and down first quarter. But talent eventually won out, and the Spurs rode another 50-point game from Wembanyama to comfortably put New Orleans away in the second half.

John Schuhmann, NBA.com — 1 (last week: 1)

OffRtg: 118.1 (4) DefRtg: 106.0 (3) NetRtg: +12.0 (1)

The Spurs' 12-game winning streak ended against the Pistons on Tuesday, when they also lost Dylan Harper to a hip strain. But they've since taken care of business against the Clippers and Pelicans; Harper's absence allows them to go back to last season's starting lineup, and they continue to have the league's best point differential.

1. The loss to Detroit was the Spurs' worst offensive game of the season, and included them scoring just 23 points on 29 possessions (79 per 100) in Victor Wembanyama's 14 minutes off the floor. Wembanyama has registered a positive plus-minus in every game since the season opener vs. the Thunder (minus-2), but the Spurs have been outscored with him on the bench in 12 of the 17. So he just went and scored 50 points (adding 18 rebounds, four steals and five blocks) in less than 32 minutes against the Pelicans on Saturday.

2. The Spurs have seen a drop in 3-point rate from last season, but that rate goes up with Julian Champagnie (the league leader in corner 3s) replacing Harper in the starting lineup. They took 56 (63%) of their 89 shots from beyond the arc, the highest rate for any team this season, in the win over New Orleans.

Coming up: The Spurs have played the league's fourth easiest schedule (cumulative opponent winning percentage of .412) thus far and will visit the Kings on Monday. After that, they'll play three games against teams — the Blazers, Lakers and Celtics — with a cumulative record of 35-15 (.700). They're 6-3 in games played between the 18 teams currently over .500, with a 22-point win in Portland included.

Law Murray, The Athletic — 1 (last week: 1)

Our alien overlord has spoken — and you know, I'm just glad that Victor Wembanyama has identified yet another issue of stat padding. Who could it be? Who needed to hear it? Which stan base will take up the shield to defend a player who absolutely doesn't need to be offended? Wemby's willingness to subliminally feed the sheep is honestly my favorite thing about him. He puts up historical, unprecedented stat lines, only to go Tim Robinson on us and inform us that we're all trying to find the guy who did this. The new man in the arena! (Also, Wemby is clearly talking about the Chicago Bulls. They're stat padding for the entire league right now. More on that later.)

Kurt Helin, NBC Sports — 1 (last week: 1)

Something to be thankful for: Victor Wembanyama's loyalty. As much as San Antonio should be thankful for Wembanyama's MVP-level start to the season — 29.9 points, 12.3 rebounds and 3.9 blocks per game — what matters even more is his Duncanesque loyalty to the Spurs. "I think this is really the ideal environment because even in other cities, where we're not the home team, people aren't as respectful. People can be very persistent or intrusive sometimes, but never here…" Wemby said postgame recently. "I'd say it's the ideal environment to spend 15 or 20 years." One other Wembanyama note: He has 72 dunks this season, which not only leads the league but is more dunks than 20 NBA teams have. He made so many it was hurting his hand, so he decided to jump-shoot it from directly above the rim (which is officially registered as a dunk).

Brett Siegel, Clutch Points — 1 (last week: 1)

Victor Wembanyama continues to prove why the San Antonio Spurs are championship favorites. At the start of November, Wemby joined Wilt Chamberlain (5x), Anthony Davis, and Bob McAdoo as the only players in NBA history with 50+ points, 15+ rebounds, and 5+ blocks in a single game. On Saturday, Wemby did it again with 50 points, 18 rebounds, and five blocks in a 114-91 win over the New Orleans Pelicans.

Only Wemby and Chamberlain have done this more than once, and with him already doing it twice this year, it seems destined that the Spurs' young star will do it again.

Even with Dylan Harper out and expected to miss at least one more week with his hip injury, the Spurs are still rolling and are the team to beat in the West. We will learn a lot about them and the Los Angeles Lakers when the two teams meet on Friday in the final game of NBA Cup group play, with the winner set to advance to the knockout round.

Mon. 11/23 at Sacramento Kings (9:00 PM CT)

Wed. 11/25 at Portland Trail Blazers (Emirates Cup, 9:00 PM CT)

Fri. 11/27 vs. Los Angeles Lakers (Emirates Cup, 8:30 PM CT)

Sun. 11/29 vs. Boston Celtics (3:00 PM CT)

Prediction: 3-1 — This is tough. The Kings game is a win (or it better be, even if Wemby and De'Aaron Fox are questionable), and they seem to have the Blazers' number lately. What worries me are those two home games, altough this is their chance for that elusive signature win. The Spurs seem to be much more focused on the road than at home this season, where they are 0-3 against contenders. Healthy Luka Doncic always seems to give them trouble, and Sunday matinees can be unpredictable, even if the Celtics are 5-5 on the road and currently on a 4-game losing streak. The Spurs especially need to win the Lakers game since it could have MVP implications (odd as that may sound this early in the season), and obviously it could be the decider of who advances to next round of the Cup Tournament.

#2615. FGCU men's basketball falls to Charlotte at the Fort Myers Tip-Off

The FGCU men's basketball team had its worst scoring performance of the season on Monday afternoon and Charlotte took advantage, handing the Eagles their fourth loss in their last five games.

FGCU lost to the 49ers 71-58 in the Eagles' first game in the 2026 Fort Myers Tip-Off at the Suncoast Credit Union Arena.

FGCU came into the game averaging 85.2 points per game as a team, shooting 37.2% from beyond the arc while averaging 12.2 three-pointers made per game. But the Eagles struggled to find their rhythm, shooting just 5-of-27 from beyond the arc, hitting less than 20% of their three-point attempts.

FGCU coach Pat Chambers was pleased with the effort to limit second chance points, restricting the opposition to just seven offensive rebounds. But while the Eagles continued to shoot from deep with little success, the 49ers flexed their strength inside to great effect, outscoring the hosts 38-22 in points inside the paint.

"We hurt ourselves, just like (we did against) FIU, FAU and (now) Charlotte," Chambers said. "There's a four-minute stretch where we turn the ball over and hurt ourselves. I thought we could compete with them, (but) those stretches are killing us... where they bust open the lead again.

"I thought we did a pretty good job in the first half, clawing back into it. (In the) second half, the first four minutes I thought we played pretty well, actually put a little fear into them, but it was the next four minutes that opened the game up."

The Eagles trailed from the outset, with Charlotte taking a quick 6-0 lead to start the game. A three from Justin Houser and a made basket from Bryson Lucas got the Eagles back within one, but the 49ers held their advantage. Charlotte's Tyler McKinley provided a spark off the bench, helping the 49ers to an 11-0 run to build a 12-point advantage midway through the half.

The Eagles did well to stay within striking distance, with a pair of three-pointers from Darren Williams providing sustenance for the Eagles' offense as they entered halftime trailing by eight.

Both teams started the second half cold, but the 49ers soon extended their lead to 12 off a three-pointer from Dorian Finister. The Eagles came back with a quick 6-0 run after a made jumper from BB Washington and a pair of transition baskets from Lucas, cutting the deficit to six with over 15 minutes still remaining in the second half.

But this would be the closest the Eagles would get as Charlotte responded with a 6-0 run of its own and continued to push. The 49ers led by as many as 20 as they cruised down the stretch to a comfortable victory.

Lucas finished with a game-high 13 points and three steals for the Eagles, with support from Williams who finished with 12 points and five rebounds. But between their inability to protect the paint or connect from beyond the arc, the Eagles had no escape routes to climb out of the hole they had dug themselves.

"(Charlotte's) defense was really good, they sped us up, but I thought we had some good (shots), they just didn't go," Chambers said. "That happens. Right now, we're a three-point shooting team, (but) we need to get some twos."

FGCU (2-5) will look to bounce back with a quick turnaround to prepare for its second game in the Fort Myers Tip-Off on Wednesday, Nov. 25, against Georgia State at 4 p.m. from Suncoast Credit Union Arena.

"Just get better, Georgia State played Charlotte already and they played great, we know they're tough and physical," Chambers said.

"You're going home right now, what you do over the next 24 hours is massive. Let's get to the trainer, let's watch the film, let's walkthrough and get ready to compete. Again, everything to me is about ASUN conference play... all this is great preparation for March."

#2614. Bears vs. Saints Game Balls: Big Jeff Dominates Again

The Chicago Bears continued their recent winning streak on Sunday afternoon, taking their fifth game in a row (and third since acquiring Jeffery Simmons). This time, it was a 21-13 victory over the New Orleans Saints at a dreary Soldier Field.

Much like last year's game against the Saints, there was some lingering rain and gray skies throughout. But that didn't take away from the newly acquired ray of sunshine on the Bears defense, Simmons, who once again was a force of nature that couldn't be denied by the opposing offensive line.

The Bears offense started hot, jumping out to a 16-0 lead by the middle of the second quarter. But much like the team's previous game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, the offense cooled off considerably down the stretch and relied upon the defense to seal the victory.

Most expected the Bears running game to get back on track in this one. The Saints came in with one of the worst rushing defenses in the league, but neither D'Andre Swift or the returning Kyle Monangai could really get things going. Salvon Ahmed, recently brought up from the practice squad, was the only running back that found much success (6 carries for 33 yards).

But the real shining star on offense on Sunday was rookie WR Thomas, who has had an up and down season thus far. Two weeks ago, he had a kick return called back on a holding penalty, while also receiving a brutal unnecessary roughness penalty (and later a fine, too) that stalled one of Chicago's scoring drives.

Thomas made up for it this week, hauling in two passes for 56 yards (tops on the team), both of them convert on third down. He also had one rush for 30 yards, and a 29-yard kickoff return.

Thomas' first catch was this 40 yard reception on 3rd and 17 early in the third quarter.

Previous to this, Thomas showcased his elite speed with a 30 yard pickup on an end-around in the second quarter.

And then there was this crucial third down conversion and wild catch in the fourth quarter.

Many Bears defenders had good outings on Sunday, but when it comes down to it, El Jefe showed why he's in the running for Defensive Player of the Year.

Simmons recorded three sacks against the Saints, taking his total up to five in his three games with Chicago so far. He finished the day with three tackles, three sacks, three tackles for loss, and three quarterback hits.

You cannot stop Jeffery Simmons. You can only hope and pray to contain him. But you'd probably fail.

It seemed as though any time the Bears needed a big stop, Simmons showed up. He now has nine sacks on the season, the most in the league among defensive tackles. As previously mentioned, he has five with the Bears through just three games. Last year, that number would have ranked third on the Bears for the season. He's done it in 12 quarters.

Simmons' total of nine for the year so far would already rank second among the 2025 squad (Montez Sweat had 10 last year), and Simmons still has seven games to go.

Honorable mentions on defense: DE Austin Booker, CB Clark Phillips III, S Dillon Thienemann, and LB Devin Bush.

#2612. Terrion Arnold's lawyer seeks sanctions for discovery violation

Seahawks cornerback Terrion Arnold remains on paid leave with eight felony charges pending in Florida. And the case against him is moving forward.

On Monday, attorney Harvey Steinberg filed a motion for sanctions, arising from the alleged failure of the prosecution to timely disclose as discovery material.

The gist of the 13-page filing is that the prosecution withheld a statement from defendant Boakai Hilton until after another defendant, Arianna Del Valle, sat for a sworn, pre-trial deposition.

Hilton’s statement, per the filing, conflicts with statements made by Del Valle. By not having the Hilton statement, Steinberg was unable to properly question Del Valle about the discrepancies.

On the surface, it’s an easy fix. Steinberg should get another chance to question Del Valle under oath. At a deeper level, the situation (if it unfolded the way Steinberg alleges) speaks to the integrity of the process.

Prosecutors have immense power and discretion, which should be exercised at all times with the goal of doing justice. Sometimes, a blind desire to “win” can overcome the mandate to do justice, thereby corrupting the system.

The Hillsborough County prosecutor’s office is already dealing with an unrelated case in which “winning” may have trumped justice. A prosecutor recently resigned for failing to disclose during jury selection that the sister of a fellow prosecutor was in the jury pool for a rape trial. The woman ultimately served as the foreperson of the jury, which returned a conviction.

A new trial has been ordered, and the prosecutor who failed to disclose the relationship has resigned.

It all comes back to integrity. Are the prosecutors playing games in order get a conviction or a favorable plea deal, or are the prosecutors committed to justice in whatever form it may take?

If Steinberg’s contentions are accurate, it looks like someone may have been trying to sandbag Arnold’s defense in an effort to make it harder to draw helpful testimony out of a key witness. In a case like this, the cards should always be on the table — with no one trying to keep anything up their sleeve.

#2609. 10 highest-graded Florida State players from loss to NC State, per PFF

Florida State hopes of securing bowl eligibility took a significant hit Saturday afternoon, as the Seminoles suffered a disappointing 28-10 Homecoming loss to NC State at Doak Campbell Stadium.

Despite carrying a 10-7 advantage into halftime, Florida State (5-6, 3-5 ACC) surrendered 21 unanswered points in the second half, falling to its sixth defeat of the season.

The Seminoles struggled on both sides of the ball, generating just 283 yards of total offense while allowing the Wolfpack to accumulate 464 yards.

Interestingly, six defensive players finished among the Seminoles' 10 highest-graded performers, despite NC State's considerable offensive production.

Here are the 10 Seminoles who stood out most in PFF's evaluations.

Lester rounded out the top 10 with a 65.0 defensive grade. Making his first career start at corner, he played 77 snaps and earned a 68.5 run-defense grade along with a 62.9 coverage grade.

Otto earned a 65.8 overall grade, including a 70.9 pass-blocking grade and a 64.2 run-blocking grade. The left guard played all 63 offensive snaps.

Parks received a 65.9 overall offensive grade, highlighted by a 73.1 rushing grade. Making his first career start, the junior led Florida State with 35 rushing yards and scored the Seminoles' only touchdown on a 9-yard run.

Brooks recorded a 67.7 defensive grade and a 73.8 tackling grade across 14 snaps. The freshman also registered his first career sack, bringing down NC State quarterback CJ Bailey for a 13-yard loss.

Pabst finished with Florida State's second-highest offensive grade at 67.9. The right tackle earned a 78.8 pass-blocking grade while playing all 63 offensive snaps.

Sanders earned a 68.3 overall grade, including a 66.9 run-defense grade. Although he played just 12 defensive snaps, his performance ranked among Florida State's best.

Kirkland made the most of his limited opportunities, earning a 68.5 defensive grade across 20 snaps. His strongest evaluation came in tackling, where he received an 80.3 grade.

Chandler was Florida State's highest-graded defensive back, recording a 69.0 overall grade and a 68.7 coverage grade. He played 81 defensive snaps, the most of any player listed in the Seminoles' defensive report.

Chaplin led Florida State's offense with a 69.8 overall grade, highlighted by an 82.4 pass-blocking grade. The left tackle played all 63 offensive snaps, continuing to provide stability along the offensive line.

LaVallee delivered the highest-graded performance of any Seminole, earning an impressive 88.4 run-defense grade and an 82.1 tackling grade across 33 defensive snaps. He was the only Florida State player to finish with an overall grade above 70.

#2605. Game Preview: Suns seek continued road success against struggling Pelicans

Who: Phoenix Suns (8-7) @ New Orleans Pelicans (5-13)

Where: Smoothie King Center — New Orleans, Louisiana

The road trip marches on as the Phoenix Suns head to the Big Easy to take on a New Orleans Pelicans team that is struggling to find its footing.

The Pelicans are 5-13 on the season, with four of those victories coming during a four-game winning streak in late October and early November. Since then, they've gone 1-9 over their last 10 games. In their most recent outing, they surrendered 50 points to Victor Wembanyama, who assured us he wasn't stat padding despite continuing to chase 50 with his team leading by 24 points in the fourth quarter.

But the Pelicans remain the conundrum they always seem to be. On paper, this is a formidable roster, one built around size and length. Three-point shooting, as expected, remains a challenge, but when you look at the personnel, there's plenty to like.

As usual, however, injuries continue to derail their potential. Trey Murphy III has appeared in only 10 games this season, starting center Yves Missi has played seven, and Dejounte Murray has suited up for nine.

Teams possessing length and defensive versatility have typically given the Suns problems over the past couple of seasons, but New Orleans has been the exception. Phoenix swept the four-game season series last year, winning by an average of 10.3 points. Granted, that margin was inflated by a 23-point victory last November, but the Suns consistently found ways to handle the Pelicans.

On the other side, Phoenix enters this matchup feeling pretty good about how its road trip has unfolded. Regardless of what happens over these final two games, the Suns have already secured a winning road trip by taking the first three. Win tonight, and you're heading into Houston feeling really good about yourself. Find a way to beat the Rockets on Wednesday, and you'll feel spectacular.

While the Suns' starting lineup still hasn't fully hit its stride, the team as a whole is beginning to look and play better. There's an opportunity to take another positive step tonight by getting quality minutes out of that starting unit against a Pelicans team that currently looks discombobulated. That's the kind of opportunity Phoenix needs to take advantage of.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Ryan Dunn — QUESTIONABLE (Right Quad Soreness)

Bennedict Mathurin — OUT (Left Plantar Fasciitis)

Bryce McGowens — PROBABLE (Left Elbow Infection)

Trey Murphy III — PROBABLE (Bilateral Patellar Tendinopathy)

Dejounte Murray — OUT (Left Ankle Sprain, Bad Attitude)

Trendon Watford — OUT (Right Ankle Sprain)

Zion Williamson — PROBABLE (Left Thigh Contusion)

Typically, I'd say I'm watching to see what the starters do, because we need that unit to gel as the season progresses if the Suns are going to become a complete basketball team capable of competing in the Western Conference. But tonight, I'm actually going to be keeping a closer eye on the bench.

While the Suns rank eighth in the NBA in bench scoring, the Pelicans rank fifth. There's talent on this New Orleans roster, of that there is no doubt. There's a reason why, whenever hypothetical trades start making the rounds, people look toward New Orleans to see which pieces they might be able to pry away. It's because the Pelicans have numerous players worth pursuing. And no, Dejounte Murray isn't one of them.

You have Saddiq Bey, Derik Queen, who could probably average a triple-double if he were starting, and Jordan Poole. I'm not suggesting the Suns should go out and acquire any of those players, but they represent quality offensive talent that can put together a run in a hurry if you're not prepared.

So while my eyes will naturally gravitate toward how effective and efficient the starting unit looks, it's the defensive intensity of Phoenix's second unit that I'll be particularly interested in watching tonight.

The Pelicans possess one of the worst defenses in the NBA, ranking 27th in defensive rating and 29th in points allowed per game. Simply put, they're going to give you opportunities to score.

So how do the Suns win this game? Beyond making sure rebounding doesn't become an issue, as it seemingly does every night, Phoenix needs to take advantage of what New Orleans gives them. The opportunities will be there. It's a matter of recognizing them, capitalizing on them, and making a struggling defense pay. Efficiency is your friend. The Pelicans allow 48.7% shooting for their opponents. Go ahead and shoot 50% tonight.

The road trip has been laden with subpar competition thus far, and to the Suns' credit, they've stepped up and taken care of business against every opponent. In fact, of the 144 minutes played on this road trip, Phoenix has trailed for only 13 of them.

I expect that trend to continue tonight, perhaps without the same level of dominance when it comes to controlling the game, but with the same result.

#2602. Colts-Texans draws tepid 12.23 million on Thursday night

NFL viewership is driven largely by quality of matchup. The Thursday night matchup that launched Week 11 may have driven some viewers to examine their life choices.

Still, 12.23 million watched the game between the Colts and Texans, on average. It was the smallest Thursday night audience of the season, and it represented a nine-percent drop from last year’s Week 11 Thursday night game between the Jets and Patriots.

The game also faced competition from the release of the new Grand Theft Auto video game.

The latest game dragged the full-season average under 17 million for the first time this year. It now stands at 16.62 million per game.

The Prime Video schedule concludes with Broncos at Steelers on Black Friday, Chiefs at Rams on December 3, Vikings at Patriots on December 10, 49ers at Chargers on December 17, Texans at Eagles on December 24, and Ravens at Bengals on December 31.

Flexing is an option for some of those contests. The window opens in Week 13, with the move “generally” coming no later than 21 days before the date of the game.

At this point, Vikings-Patriots is only 17 days away. Flexing remains possible for the final three games — with 49ers-Chargers possibly on the brink of being moved. The Week 15 Sunday schedule otherwise includes Bengals-Panthers, Browns-Giants, and Cowboys-Rams.
