#2600. NFL news, live updates: Micah Parsons reportedly set to return, 49ers waive Brandon Aiyuk

Week 11 of the NFL season is almost completed with the Cincinnati Bengals (7-2) and Washington Commanders (3-5-1) set to play on Monday night.

The week began with Thursday night's matchup between the Indianapolis Colts and Houston Texans. The Texans prevailed 26-3 as the Colts performed so poorly offensively that the team decided to move on from quarterback Daniel Jones and released him the next day.

Jones' next destination will be one of the NFL's top stories as Week 11 finishes and the season progresses toward Week 12. The eight-year veteran is expected to clear waivers on Monday and from there, he will wait to see what his best option is, according to ESPN. Among the teams that could line up to sign him are the Arizona Cardinals, New England Patriots and Baltimore Ravens.

The San Francisco 49ers defeated the Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City to close out Sunday's slate. With the 35-10 victory, the Niners improved to 9-1 to keep hold of the top seed in the NFC. The Buffalo Bills matched them for the NFL's best mark with a 26-9 win over the Miami Dolphins.

One concern for NFL coaches this week might be players celebrating touchdowns with backflips and front-flips. New York Giants running back Cam Skattebo and Detroit Lions receiver Jameson Williams each went airborne in their Week 11 games, surely making their head coaches cringe. Teams have seven more weeks to keep their players on the ground.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for the latest NFL news, injury updates and more coming out of Week 11.

After missing nearly a year with tears to his ACL and meniscus, Micah Parsons is set to return to the Packers' defensive line this week. The star linebacker has reportedly been cleared to play as of Monday, per NFL Network's Ian Rapoport.

Parsons reportedly needed one final clearance on his knee before getting the green light, and will now make his debut against the Rams on Wednesday.

New York Giants cornerback Greg Newsome will be sidelined for "multiple weeks" with a high ankle sprain, head coach John Harbaugh confirmed on Monday.

It's still unclear if Newsome will need surgery or if he will land on injured reserve, which would knock him out for at least four games.

"It's a serious high ankle. It's gonna be one of those lengthy high ankles," Harbaugh said, via ESPN. "It's going to be multiple weeks."

For more on Newsome's injury, click here.

San Francisco is finally parting ways with wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk.

After a months-long saga around the receiver wanting to be released, Aiyuk was officially waived by the 49ers on Monday. That confirms reports earlier on Monday indicating that Aiyuk would finally be released.

Other teams will have until Wednesday to claim Aiyuk, who will become a free agent after he clears waivers.

Former Colts quarterback Daniel Jones was not claimed on Monday, thus making him a free agent.

Jones is free to sign with any team that wants him, and that team can pay him the veteran minimum.

The Colts still owe Jones about $20 million.

The Commanders have activated running back Jacory Croskey-Merritt from injured reserve, and he is off the injury report.

JCM will play Monday night against the Bengals. He hasn't played since Week 5 due to a groin injury.

So far this season, Croskey-Merritt has 239 yards and two touchdowns on 67 carries. He also has 40 yards on five receptions.

The Cardinals have reportedly fired defensive coordinator Nick Rallis, per NFL Network's Ian Rapoport.

Arizona's defense has allowed 355.2 yards per game this season, the seventh-most in the league. The Cardinals' allowing 29.4 points per game is the second-most in the league, trailing only the Raiders at 29.7 points per game.

San Francisco 49ers general manager John Lynch has agreed to a multi-year contract extension with the team, according to reports.

Lynch and team owner Jed York agreed to the new contract with a handshake while on the sideline at Estadio Banorte before Sunday night's 35-10 win over the Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City, Fox Sports' Jay Glazer reported.

Exact terms of Lynch's extension have not yet been reported. The 49ers have not officially announced the agreement. Lynch's current contract was set to expire after this season.

Lynch's new deal joins an extension for head coach Kyle Shanahan, who reached agreement on the contract in September shortly after the 49ers played the Los Angeles Rams in Melbourne, Australia. San Francisco's international games this season have been extremely lucrative for the team's top football authority figures.

Rams head coach Sean McVay said Monday that tight end Terrance Ferguson will need season-ending surgery on his ankle.

Ferguson is the latest Rams player to be dealing with a long-term injury. Wide receiver Puka Nacua will also miss the remainder of the season due to a knee injury. Davante Adams has been sidelined since Week 6, though he is expected back by mid-December.

Ferguson's season ends with him playing in just three games. He finished with 63 yards and a touchdown on seven receptions.

The San Francisco 49ers are reportedly releasing veteran wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk, per ESPN's Adam Schefter.

Aiyuk missed the entire 2025 NFL season after recovering from a knee injury he suffered in 2024, just months after signing a four-year, $120 million extension with the team. He suffered a torn ACL and MCL in late October 2024 and hasn't played a snap in the league since.

After being placed on the physically unable to perform (PUP) list to start the year, reports later emerged that Aiyuk failed to participate in team meetings, and the Niners were frustrated with the "lack of communication." Eventually, he was placed on the Reserve/Left Squad list in December due to his continued absence and lack of communication with the team.

If the New England Patriots are interested in adding quarterback Daniel Jones, head coach Mike Vrabel isn't saying so.

While Jones waits to clear waivers, the Patriots named Tommy DeVito as their starting QB for Week 12's matchup with the Los Angeles Chargers. However, even if New England signed Jones, he would be unlikely to take the field with them so soon after joining the team.

Taking over after Drake Maye was injured in Week 10's game versus the Detroit Lions, DeVito completed 12 of 24 passes for 81 yards with 1 touchdown and 1 interception.

"We're excited about that, good preseason, and Tommy's improved," Vrabel said at his Monday press conference.

"I know that going into the last game, I think that being thrown in there and had some good plays, had some things that he can improve on and little things," he added. "I think that was probably the biggest thing is just saying, 'Hey, getting back into game action. How do you work in the pocket?' These are things that he got so much better on when he got here."

Green Bay Packers linebacker Micah Parsons "did well" in Sunday's padded practice session, according to coach Matt LaFleur.

However, the team will wait until after Tuesday's practice to determine if Parsons can play in Wednesday's matchup with the Los Angeles Rams.

The status of offensive lineman Sean Rhyan (high ankle sprain) and linebacker Zaire Franklin (shin) are also still to be determined. Rhyan has been out since Week 7, while Franklin has been sidelined since Week 6.

Running back Josh Jacobs (fractured finger) is unlikely to play Wednesday, but is doing individual drills to maintain conditioning, LaFleur said.

Fernando Mendoza's first career NFL touchdown pass is now in the books.

The Las Vegas Raiders quarterback connected with running back Ashton Jeanty for a deep touchdown in the second quarter of their matchup with the Denver Broncos on Sunday afternoon. Mendoza found Jeanty open up the right side of the field for what ended up being an easy 68-yard score at Empower Field.

The touchdown brought the Raiders within one touchdown of the Broncos just ahead of halftime.

Naturally, Mendoza was extremely excited.

#2599. Luka Dončić gave a ‘strong endorsement’ of Quentin Grimes before free agency

Two seasons ago, Quentin Grimes and Luka Dončić shared the floor for 21 games and 228 minutes as members of the Dallas Mavericks before Dončić got injured and the two were ultimately both traded away in separate deals. During those minutes, Dallas outscored its opponents by 37 points.

Apparently, that was all the time and success necessary for Grimes to earn Dončić's trust as the Slovenian superstar pushed for the Los Angeles Lakers to sign him in free agency this past summer, according to Marc Stein.

"League sources say that Grimes, like new Lakers big man Walker Kessler, received a very strong endorsement from Dončić when the Lakers were assembling their offseason plans to make the 26-year-old one of their primary free agent targets," Stein wrote in his Substack, The Stein Line.

Aside from having Dončić in his corner, Grimes was also backed by head coach JJ Redick. Those are a couple of pretty powerful allies.

"And that gave Grimes two significant purple-and-gold advocates: Face of the Franchise and Lakers coach JJ Redick," Stein wrote. "There was confidence within the organization that Grimes could not only bring a trusted catch-and shoot prowess to Lakerland but also play more consistently closer to the level that resulted in the 6 foot-4 guard averaging 21.9 points per game for Philadelphia across the final 28 games of the 2024-25 season."

Thus far, that confidence has been rewarded. Following a slower start to the year, Grimes is playing excellently, averaging 12.7 points, 3.5 rebounds and 2.9 assists. He's shooting 41.2% beyond the arc and 56.9% on 2-pointers, and dropped a career-high 47 points in a win over the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday.

Since joining the starting lineup in place of an injured Austin Reaves four games ago, he's averaging 25.5 points, 6.0 rebounds and 3.8 assists while shooting 57.1% from deep. That ability to scale up offensively is affirming the organization's confidence in him replicating "the level that resulted in the 6 foot-4 guard averaging 21.9 points per game for Philadelphia across the final 28 games of the 2024-25 season," as Stein reported.

Grimes and the Lakers return to the floor Monday night against the Utah Jazz at 7 p.m.

#2597. Bears have gone five straight games without ever trailing for first time since 1985

The Bears’ win over the Saints on Sunday was their fifth consecutive game taking a first-quarter lead and winning without ever trailing. That gives this Bears team an accomplishment the franchise hasn’t achieved in 41 years.

The last Bears team to win five straight games without ever trailing was the 1985 Bears, one of the most dominant teams in NFL history.

That 1985 Bears team lost just one game, a Monday night classic against the Dolphins in the 13th game of the season, and after that loss to the Dolphins they never trailed in the final three games of the regular season, or in the two playoff games they won to earn a trip to Super Bowl XX. In the Super Bowl, the Bears fell behind 3-0 in the first quarter before winning 46-10, snapping their streak of five straight games without trailing.

This Bears team isn’t dominating quite like that 1985 team, but the Bears are currently on a very impressive run: They haven’t just won five games in a row, but they’ve scored in every quarter of all five games, the franchise’s first streak of 20 consecutive quarters scoring since the 1941 Bears. That team, like the 1985 team, won the NFL championship.

The 2026 Bears are playing like some of the best teams in franchise history. They’re jumping out to early leads and never looking back.

#2595. Florida State hires Glenn Schumann as next head football coach

Florida State has hired Georgia defensive coordinator Glenn Schumann as the program's next head football coach.

Both Schumann and FSU met on Sunday, Nov. 22, as both sides worked to finalize an agreement. After continued discussion, the decision was made for Schumann to become the Seminoles' next head coach. His hire was announced by ESPN's Pete Thamel on Monday, Nov. 23,

The 36-year-old joins the Seminoles program after a decade of coaching at Georgia in various roles, most recently serving as the Bulldogs' defensive coordinator. He is one of the fastest-rising coaches in the collegiate football world and has extensive experience under two of the sport's most respected coaches.

He joined the top-ranked Bulldogs and Kirby Smart's staff after seven seasons as a defensive staffer at Alabama under Nick Saban, where he worked alongside now-FSU athletic director Jeff Purinton. With Georgia, Schumann won two national titles in 2021 and 2022. He was co-defensive coordinator in 2021 and was the defensive coordinator and primary play caller for the 2022 national title.

Schumann became the focus of the program's search fairly early in the process, despite the Valdosta, Georgia, native not having head coaching experience, something that the university reportedly preferred for its next coach. Despite that, Schumann wowed both Smart and Saban during his time on the respective staffs and earned a reputation for being an elite recruiter and developer of talent.

He has cultivated multiple NFL Draft picks, including seven selections in the 2025 NFL Draft, and coached multiple award winners, including Butkus Award (top linebacker in the nation) winners Roquan Smith, Nakobe Dean, and Jalon Walker.

Schumann helped contribute to a top-10 recruiting class in every season he was at Georgia, including signing Tallahassee native Class of 2023 5-star linebacker Raylen Wilson. During his time, he oversaw three No. 1 overall recruiting classes, including back-to-back top-ranked classes in 2024 and 2025. The Class of 2027 Georgia recruiting class currently ranks fifth in the country.

Under Schumann, Georgia ranks sixth in the nation in total defense, allowing 267 yards per game. He will be tasked with revitalizing an FSU defense that currently allows 407.7 yards per game, ranked 117th out of 138 teams in the country.

The announcement of his hire comes after the Seminoles' disastrous 28-10 Homecoming loss to North Carolina State. FSU allowed 464 yards of total offense, including 205 yards of total offense from running back Davion Gause (134 rushing, 71 receiving yards).

Schumann will be one of the youngest head coaches in the country, and he takes over a program that is craving success.

The Tallahassee Democrat understands that FSU aims to put $35-$40 million towards next year's roster. Of the millions put toward the roster, a significant chunk is expected to go to a quarterback, a position that the university has struggled to find stability in over the past decade.

One of the biggest, and most immediate questions that Schumann will need to answer is who his offensive coordinator will be. A defensive guru, the offensive coordinator hire will be one of the most critical for the first-year head coach.

2008-11 | Alabama (undergraduate analyst)

2014-15 | Alabama (director of player development/associate director of player personnel)

2019-22 | Georgia (Co-Defensive Coord./Inside LBs)

2022-2026 | Georgia (Defensive Coord./Inside LBs)

#2592. Micah Parsons cleared to play Wednesday night

Micah Parsons is set to make his long-awaited return to the Packers lineup against the Rams on Wednesday night.

Parsons said on Monday that he was waiting to speak with Dr. Neal ElAttrache, who performed his ACL surgery, later in the day about whether he'd get the green light. Ian Rapoport of NFL Media reports that call has taken place and that Parsons has received final medical clearance to make his first appearance of the 2026 season.

The defensive end has been practicing for the last few weeks and has now moved through every step of his return from the torn ACL he suffered last December.

Parsons joined the Packers in a trade ahead of the 2025 season and had 12.5 sacks in 14 games before that injury. It remains to be seen how much Parsons will play in his first game in nearly a year, but the Packers will be hoping for him to have an impact in whatever playing time he gets Wednesday while looking for that to ramp up as he regains his form in the coming weeks.

#2589. Indianapolis Colts have quarterback tryouts to back up Anthony Richardson

The Indianapolis Colts need a third quarterback, so they're working out a few ahead of their Week 12 game against the New York Giants.

Seth Henigan was on the Colts' active roster late last season and the Jacksonville Jaguars' practice squad early in 2025. He hasn't taken an NFL regular-season snap. He played for Memphis from 2021-23, passing for 14,266 yards, 104 touchdowns and 31 interceptions.

Clayton Tune played for the Arizona Cardinals and Green Bay Packers from 2023-25, appearing in 15 games and making two starts. He has passed for 112 yards with no touchdowns and 3 interceptions. From 2018-22, he played for Houston, throwing for 11,996 yards, 104 TDs and 41 INTs.

Bailey Zappe played for the New England Patriots and Cleveland Browns from 2022-24, starting 9 games and passing for 2,223 yards, 12 TDs and 14 INTs. He has also been with the Kansas City Chiefs. In 2021 at Western Kentucky, he passed for 5,967 yards, 62 TDs and 11 INTs.

#2587. Daniel Jones clears waivers, becomes free agent

As expected, former Colts quarterback Daniel Jones has cleared waivers. He’s now a free agent.

The same thing happened two years ago, after the Giants benched Jones for the same financial reasons (i.e., to avoid the vesting of injury guarantees) and then released him at his request.

Two years ago, Jones eventually joined the Vikings’ practice squad. And joining a practice squad seems like a viable option again, unless he prefers to remain truly free and clear until the planets perfectly align for him to join a contender and start playing.

He’ll continue to be paid by the Colts because his full 2026 salary is guaranteed. Given the rollercoaster ride he has experienced this year in Indianapolis, at this point he may just want to take a break.

Still, as we’ve seen time and again, there aren’t enough good quarterbacks to go around. Even if it’s debatable, based on his performance in 2026, whether he remains a “good quarterback.”

#2586. Dan Campbell provides promising update after Kerby Joseph’s 1st game back

Last week, Detroit Lions coach Dan Campbell said the real tell about Kerby Joseph's return to the lineup will be how he feels after the game. The knee injury that held him out for over a year wasn't going to be as much of a problem on Sunday as it may be after playing on it for three hours.

But Campbell provided some great news on Monday. The Lions coach said Joseph came out of Sunday's win over the Tampa Bay Buccaneers with no cause for concern the day after.

"I think pretty good here. There's no red flags," Campbell said. "So that's a good sign."

Joseph played 46 defensive snaps on Sunday as part of Detroit's three-safety looks vs. the Bucs. He regularly manned the deep section of the field, while Brian Branch dropped down to nickel, and veteran Chuck Clark roamed the entirety of the field. While Joseph finished with just two tackles, Campbell said there were ups and downs to his afternoon.

"He'll be better for those reps, just like Branch was, to get his first reps of the season," Campbell said. "So, I thought he did some good things. There are things that he's going to only just get better at now that he's been out there. I thought it was pretty good. When you're able to get he and BB on the field and you've got Chuck out there, it's a pretty good unit."

After the game, Joseph told reporters he felt no discomfort in his knee for the entirety of the game.

"Every time I come out here, I feel like myself," Joseph said. "I don't know why you keep asking that. I'm going to always be me. I'm going to forever be me."

At this point, it appears Joseph is on track to play on a short week against the Chicago Bears, but we'll know more when the team drops their first injury report of the week on Monday. Detroit is only expected to conduct walkthroughs this week, so participation levels will be estimated.

But rest and recovery are a close second on any NFL team's priority list, so practice was a walkthrough. That means this list is just a best-guess by the training staff.

*Oliver wasn't on last week's daily reports but did report soreness on Saturday and was added to the list at that time.

The good news in this group is that most of these would have participated even if it had been a full practice day. Benford is the only player listed as a DNP at all. Williams is recovering as is Kincaid.

He returned to play in the second half. Not being on the list – with either the shoulder injury he's been dealing with since Week 2 or a knee injury – is a great sign for the Bills' offense.

#2578. NBA viewership off to record start, national games up 37% from last season

NBA viewership is off to a record start this season.

The league announced Monday that viewership over the first month of the 2026-27 campaign is up 20% from last year on nationally broadcast games on NBC, ESPN and Amazon. National games are averaging a record 2.62 million viewers, too, which is up about 37% from last season.

The numbers are a solid increase for the league as it enters the second year of a new massive 11-year media rights deal, which will run through the 2035-36 season. The new deal extended games with ABC and ESPN, brought national games back to NBC and Amazon Prime Video entered the fold, too. Roughly 75 regular-season games are set to be broadcast on national TV each season, up from just the minimum 15 under the previous agreement.

The league also announced that viewership of live games on NBA League Pass and through the NBA app is up 50%. NBA Cup games on Prime Video are breaking records, too. Those tournament games are averaging about 2 million viewers each so far this season, which is up 90% from last season. The NBA Cup is in its fourth season of existence.

The viewership spike comes after a massive showing during the NBA Finals last season. The series between the New York Knicks and San Antonio Spurs averaged roughly 20.6 million views on ABC and ESPN, which made it the most-watched NBA Finals in nearly three decades.

Viewership numbers are calculated through Nielsen, which shifted to a new methodology in recent years that pairs both big data from smart TV and traditional panel boxes.

While the season still has a long way to go to live up to the NBA Finals numbers that the Knicks and Spurs drew last spring, it's clear that the league's new media deal is off to a very impressive start.

#2574. Report: NFL Primetime documentary is in the works

The recent passing of Tom Jackson has sparked much discussion about ESPN’s NFL Primetime, which was for years a mandatory Sunday evening destination for football fans everywhere.

As it turns out, a documentary is coming regarding the show.

Via Sam Neumann of Awful Announcing, the final product is believed to be on track for a release in connection with the Super Bowl, which is being televised by ESPN and ABC.

It could be part of the game-day programming. Three years ago, CBS debuted You Are Looking Live! — a documentary about The NFL Today — before the launch of the official pregame show in advance of Super Bowl LVIII.

For those who were actively following the NFL in the late ‘80s, NFL Primetime provided the first opportunity to see extended highlights of NFL games on the day they were played. Years before the Internet, social media, Sunday Ticket, and RedZone, Primetime became appointment viewing for fans who wanted to see more about what was happening beyond the two or three games available in their local markets on Sunday afternoon.

And the team of Chris Berman and Tom Jackson became the tour guides for the opportunity to essentially watch all of the important plays from every game in a compressed but meaningful package, with Berman’s cadence and the music accompanying the words creating the loose impression that it was all happening live. Even though everyone knew it wasn’t.

It became a unique moment in time, which at the time didn’t seem to have a shelf life. It’s just the way things were, with fans having no concept that there would ever be a way to watch all of the games live or to see any and all relevant plays long before 7:00 p.m. ET. It was a new frontier that no one had reason to believe would not remain a permanent fixture of each and every NFL Sunday.

As Neumann explains it, Jackson recently made a surprise return to ESPN as part of the filming of the documentary. ESPN shared footage of the reunion on Sunday.

It’s worth your time to watch it, if you were one of the millions who climbed aboard the Primetime Express every Sunday night of football season for something much more meaningful than a glimpse of the entire day of NFL action. And the documentary will undoubtedly bring back fond memories of the final years before advances in technology caused a narrow range of football options to explode into today’s endless menu of choices.

#2571. Grizzlies rookie forward Cameron Boozer to miss at least a month with sprained right ankle

MEMPHIS, Tenn. (AP) — Memphis Grizzlies rookie forward Cameron Boozer has a sprained right ankle that will sideline him for at least a month.

Boozer was injured during the third quarter of a 126-98 victory over the Golden State Warriors on Thursday.

The Grizzlies announced Monday that Boozer underwent imaging that revealed a moderate-grade medial sprain. The team said additional testing confirmed the ankle is structurally stable.

Boozer will be re-evaluated in four weeks.

Boozer has averaged 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds and 5.3 assists through 16 games. The Grizzlies selected the 6-foot-8 forward out of Duke with the third pick in the NBA draft.

#2570. Mike LaFleur: Defense needed a fresh voice

Cardinals head coach Mike LaFleur made a significant change to his coaching staff on Monday.

Defensive coordinator Nick Rallis was dismissed a day after the Cardinals gave up 435 yards in a 34-7 loss to the Chiefs. They lost 34-6 to the Rams the prior week and LaFleur said the team is trying to find solutions for a unit that hasn’t “stopped the bleeding enough.

“I just felt in my heart this morning we needed a change,” LaFleur said, via the team’s website. “A fresh voice.”

LaFleur acknowledged that an offense that’s scored 16 points while starting three different quarterbacks in the last three weeks hasn’t “held our end of the bargain,” but the team has not made any coaching changes on that side of the ball

Senior defensive assistant Teryl Austin will take on the defensive coordinator role on an interim basis. Lafleur said he will “put his own spin” on how the team plays on that side of the ball over the final six games.

#2569. Today's top games to watch, best bets, odds: Dodgers vs. Brewers, Bills vs. Rams on MNF and more

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The Milwaukee Brewers did just enough against Tarik Skubal to draw first blood in the 2026 National League Championship Series. If they hope to take a 2-0 lead in their matchup with the two-time reigning world champion Los Angeles Dodgers, they must find a way to get to a pitcher who has been spectacular for most of his postseason career.

Make your MLB, Monday Night Football and NHL picks at BetMGM Sportsbook with promo code CBSSPORTS and get up to $1,500 in bonus bets if your first bet loses:

Milwaukee continues its quest for the second World Series appearance in franchise history when it hosts Yoshinobu Yamamoto and the Dodgers at 5 p.m. ET in Game 2 of the 2026 NLCS at American Family Field. The Brewers, who were swept by Los Angeles in this round last year, made their only trip to the Fall Classic in 1982 and lost to the St. Louis Cardinals in seven games while residing in the American League.

The Dodgers took the lead in Game 1 on Andy Pages' RBI single in the second inning, but Brice Turang led off the fourth against Skubal with his second career postseason homer and Luis Lara put the Brewers ahead in the following frame with a run-scoring base hit. It proved to be enough for Milwaukee as relievers Abner Uribe, Aaron Ashby and Trevor Megill kept Los Angeles off the board over the final four innings.

Owners of the best record in the major leagues during the regular season (103-59), the Brewers now must solve Yamamoto, who is 8-1 with a 2.14 ERA in 11 playoff games -- 10 starts -- since joining the Dodgers in 2024. The 28-year-old Japanese right-hander has allowed fewer than two runs in eight of his 11 overall postseason appearances and seven of the last eight.

Yamamoto went 5-1 with a 1.45 ERA and two complete games in last year's playoffs and was named World Series MVP after recording three victories against the Toronto Blue Jays. One of those complete games came in Game 2 of the 2025 NLCS, as he limited the Brewers to three hits and one run while registering seven strikeouts. Yamamoto was dominant in his debut this postseason, holding the Atlanta Braves to one run and four hits while fanning 10 batters over seven innings in Game 3 of their NL Division Series.

Milwaukee counters with rookie Logan Henderson, who made his first career playoff appearance in Game 2 of the 2026 NLDS against the San Diego Padres and allowed two runs -- one earned -- and two hits across five innings of a no-decision. The 24-year-old righty won both of his starts versus the Dodgers during the regular season, permitting one run and five hits with 13 strikeouts over 12 innings. Yamamoto was effective against the Brewers this year, as well, going 2-0 while giving up two runs and fanning 12 across 13 frames in two outings.

The 2026 ALCS begins Monday as the Cleveland Guardians visit Tropicana Field in St. Petersburg, Fla. to take on the Tampa Bay Rays at 8 p.m. ET. Left-hander Foster Griffin makes the start for Cleveland, which lost the first two games of the ALDS at home before posting three consecutive victories over the Chicago White Sox.

Tampa Bay turns to righty Drew Rasmussen, who was four outs away from a no-hitter in Game 1 of the Rays' three-game sweep of the New York Yankees in the ALDS and finished with 10 strikeouts across eight scoreless innings. Griffin did not face Tampa Bay this season, while Rasmussen went 1-1 with a 1.50 ERA and fanned 15 over 12 frames in two starts against the Guardians.

#2568. Robert Saleh has ‘100 percent support’ of owners during 1-9 Titans season

Robert Saleh has no concerns about support from the Titans' ownership despite a 1-9 record to start the season.

Speaking with reporters on Monday, he said that he has "100 percent support" from Titans owner Amy Adams Strunk and down through the organization during his first season with the team. The declaration comes a day after the Titans fell to the Cowboys 51-30 and their only win has come against the Colts back in Week 6.

"There's an expectation to get better, and this is coming from me and nobody else," Saleh said, according to the Associated Press. "And I'll tell you guys the same thing I tell the coaches and I tell players, that the expectation is that you find a way to get better and the people who get better will stay. The people who don't get better, will not."

Tennessee Titans head coach Robert Saleh walks off the field after a game against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Saleh has been lauded for his work with defenses in the past, but the Titans' defense has been bad this season, giving up at least 40 points in three straight games following Sunday's loss, and Tennessee has allowed opposing teams to score on 13 consecutive drives.

The Titans are 30th in the NFL and have allowed 29 points a game on average and are last in yards allowed per game, averaging of 399.8.

Saleh called Sunday's loss "disgusting, embarrassing football" after the game and he was still plenty critical when he addressed the media on Monday.

"Our young guys are getting a lot of play. We're getting better in some areas in that regard, but these last couple of weeks, I'm not going to try to sugarcoat anything, it's been dogs–t," Saleh said bluntly.

Things won't get any easier this upcoming weekend when the Titans have to face the highly potent Jaguars offense in Week 12 on the road at EverBank Stadium in Jacksonville. The Jaguars are coming off a disappointing 23-18 loss to the Giants on Sunday.

Daniel Jones wasn’t the only veteran to become a free agent on Monday.

#2566. Jon Sumrall knows Florida State rivalry is not just another game

Jon Sumrall understands that Friday's trip to Tallahassee carries implications extending far beyond Florida's eighth victory of the season.

The Gators will close the regular season against Florida State after securing their first winning SEC record since 2020. Sumrall discussed the importance of controlling an in-state rivalry, the message Urban Meyer shared with him and how he is teaching Florida's transfers what the matchup means to the program's supporters.

Sumrall also evaluated the offensive line, Aaron Philo and Jadan Baugh, addressed Florida's need for additional roster resources and explained how the Gators are navigating a short week before their Black Friday finale.

All right. Good to get a win last week. Didn't play particularly well early in the game in a lot of areas.

We got into halftime, and I thought our guys responded out of the half the right way. A lot of sloppy things that we put on tape still show we're a long way away from being a complete football team, but we found a way to win. That's what matters.

I think Vanderbilt is probably a better team than its record and better than people realize. I thought they played really well together. I think they have an identity. You can tell they know how they want to play the football game.

It was a challenge. Glad we were able to get the win.

Rivalry week. Big opportunity for us. A talented Florida State team with athletes all over the field. In these games, you've got to be ready for anything.

Short week, so today was really a Tuesday practice for us. Yesterday was sort of a Monday with less pace to it, but the same type of work. The guys will have Saturday off after the game. That will be the day off this week.

It's better than six. It's not as good as eight or nine or whatever.

I'm so micro right now and in the moment that — I want to win every game we play. I also haven't shied away from the fact that, as we're growing as a program and trying to build the foundation, we can't shy away from milestones that show growth.

That may mean the first winning conference record in several years, since 2020. That's a big deal. In order for us to go where we want to go long term, that's not the end goal. But there has been some growth.

Trailing at halftime the last several games and seeing a different mindset compared to the first couple, and how we've responded at times, has been growth. I'm glad there are a lot of things we've accomplished. We've missed a lot of opportunities as well.

I'm probably such a realist that I don't feel really excited about winning seven games. I also understand the good in that, too. But I'm not like, "Man, we've accomplished so much."

I feel a little frustrated with some of the missed opportunities we've had, but we'll get there. There has been growth in this football team. It hasn't been as fast as I wanted at times, but there has been growth.

We still have areas where we have to grow a lot to get where we want to go long term here.

The frustration is just the lack of consistency. There are flashes of good play, and there are flashes of awful play.

Some of that at times has to do with being an SEC player and playing really good fronts. But at the same time, there are times when we don't have enough things blocked where we get the ball carrier back to the line of scrimmage and help him.

I've always heard this — I think I'm in my 22nd or something year coaching college football now — it's the offensive line's job to make sure the running back gets back to the line of scrimmage, and it's the running back's job to do something from there.

At times, we have not done a good enough job of getting the ball carrier back to the line. That pisses me off. That drives me nuts.

Protection is the same deal. We've had times this year when we've protected the passer well, and then we've had some major missed opportunities to get the ball downfield because we didn't have enough time in the pocket.

There have been some good moments. There have been some bad moments — more bad moments than I would like. It's a frustrating deal because the offensive linemen care. Phil Trautwein cares deeply. He's passionate about getting this right. Everybody on our offensive staff does.

I've said this before: The offensive line is the one position that needs nobody's help to do its job well. It's also the one position everybody needs to do well so they can do their jobs well.

The quarterback, running backs and receivers all need the offensive line to play well. The offensive line needs nobody else to play well for it to play well.

I think the thing that has changed within Aaron as much as anything is, when you have the run he had the first several games and it doesn't go well, then you end up standing on the sideline for a little bit to watch it, I think when you get your opportunity again, you're like, "I'm going to lay it all on the line. I'm going to do whatever it takes."

You have felt the edge he has played with. The mentality he has played with has made our entire football team better.

At times, yeah, I think we've used him a little bit more in the designed run game. But I also think when it hasn't been there, he has understood, "Hey, I may have to tuck it and go get what's there."

The biggest thing for him has been understanding when he needs to slide and when he doesn't, and understanding the down-and-distance situation. If it's a third-down play, there is no sliding. You go get it.

I think it has sped him up at times, maybe, but the rush can do that to you. We need to make it cleaner in the pocket for him. We'd be able to operate and do some things downfield in the throwing game better than we've been able to do.

That's probably the thing we have not been able to get to as much as we would like: some of the shots. Those require time. If we don't give him time, it doesn't matter if a guy can run fast and get open. We have to hold up and have a clean pocket.

It's a big deal. You have a lot of respect for everybody we play in the state, or even the ones we don't, but you want to win the in-state battles, whether it's on the field, in recruiting or whatever. We compete every day, so it's a huge deal.

Coach Meyer texted me, gosh, maybe around the Texas game. He just said, "Hey, finish this thing strong all the way down the stretch of November. Recruit the roster you need to recruit to build this thing."

He emphasized finishing November for a lot of reasons and getting momentum as you go into the offseason and build for the future. This game is a big part of it. In-state rivalries matter. Anybody who says it's just another game, I don't buy into that. It's not just another game. It's an in-state rivalry. Those are different.

In the world we live in, they're all big because we play in the SEC, and week in and week out, they're all big games and challenging games. But this is one that means something to so many people because of the in-state rivalry component. It's a bragging-rights and in-state game, which does matter.

I've done my research on it to some degree. I like to educate myself on the history and all the things that go along with it.

When this game was played last year, we were playing at Tulane a little bit later in the day. I might have watched most of the game with a pretty keen eye because I had a good idea of what I was going to do next.

I've grown up watching college football my whole life. These games are the games that, as a kid, you always had the TV on and were ready to watch. Florida-Florida State is a big game.

Some of the stuff that was working early for us didn't work at times in the run game. He dealt with some personal adversity in regard to his success on the field. It didn't quite look like it did earlier in the year.

You grow through that. It can either break you, or you can own it and improve from it. He has owned it and improved. I think he has done a better job lately of trying to stick his foot in the ground and get north and south.

When you're as good a player as Jadan is, sometimes there's this tendency where you want every play to be explosive. You want every run to be a "wow" run.

Sometimes, as a running back, a 4-yard gain is a "wow" run. He had a couple of those Saturday. He had one where he made a 5-yard gain out of nothing that should've been a negative-four-yard play. Even when we get him back to the line the right way, it's understanding that a 4- or 5-yard gain is a good play.

He has had enough "wow" runs that have been 20-yard runs that he naturally wants all of them to look like that. That would be great, too. But as you mature as a back and prepare yourself long term to play this game, hopefully for a long time at the next level, I think you understand when you watch pro football that a 5-yard run is a good run.

Our league is the closest thing I think you get to the NFL in college football. Learning that sometimes 4- or 5-yard runs are great runs, he has grown a lot.

He has had some frustrating moments this year. He has had some real highs as well, and he stayed the course. I'm proud of him and grateful for what he has contributed to this football team and program.

I didn't know the bowl-stat tidbit, but anytime you have the chance to be the first person to do something in a program like the University of Florida, that's special. That's unique. This is one of those storied programs.

It's funny, I was talking to Brad White early this morning about how there are certain programs with so much tradition that even the uniform — I'm a traditionalist. I like orange helmets, blue jerseys, white pants, and orange helmets, white jerseys and either white, blue or orange pants on the road.

This is one of those places with so much tradition, history and pageantry around the program that, as a player, when you have a chance in 2026 to do something that has not been done at a place like this, that's hard to find.

You go through a lot of our history, and there are very few things where you go, "That has never been done."

For him to have a crack at doing something that has not been done means something. It's a special opportunity for him to pursue. We need to play well for him to accomplish it.

First and foremost, Gary Condron is an unbelievable supporter. Our indoor facility has his name on it. He's one of the most consistent supporters of all Florida athletics, but he has been unbelievable to our program in every way.

There's a group around him, other people who are very committed to our success. Chris LaFace and a bunch of people — you guys know all the names. I'm not hiding any of them.

I need to call Gary and see what winning an eighth game would do to the Condron Club. How much more would that get?

We need great resourcing. I'm not going to shy away from the fact that if you have championship expectations, you have championship resourcing.

We do have great resourcing in so many regards here already. There's a lot of good here. Can we make it better? Yeah, we can make it better. My job is trying to do everything I can to push to make it better.

I think, in fairness, there were probably people who wanted to see at least some proof of concept, to make sure I wasn't an idiot and knew at least how to get 11 guys on the field, run a formation, win a close game or do some things that matter to building a program.

I think we've shown some of that at times. We've beaten three ranked teams this year — two on the road and three total, I believe, or something. Y'all know those stats better than I do.

There has been some proof of concept to some degree. We've also failed miserably at times. But if we want to be a top-10 team year in and year out, we need to pull everybody on board so we can be resourced in the top 10 financially with our roster.

We've got great support here. Scott Stricklin and the whole group in the UAA work their tails off to help us get everything we can get to be successful.

I'm a firm believer that less isn't more. More is more. We need to try to get into that top group in our conference if we want to consistently be a championship-level team, which we're going to get there.

I'm grateful for the resources we've got. I also want to push to be one of those teams that puts itself in the group where it's like, "Hey, we don't just hope we can win. We should win, or you should run me out of here."

I think it's really important, regardless of what your resourcing is, to be good stewards of it. I don't want more money just to go out and frivolously spend it on crazy things. I want us to be really thoughtful.

The good news for me is, selfishly, I don't do the contracts. I know what we're doing. I'm involved in all of that. But we've got an unbelievable front office here with Dave Caldwell, Nick Polk and that whole crew. They bring me in and go, "Hey, Coach, will you watch these eight players?"

I watch them and rank them one through eight. They go, "OK, that one is going to force us to do that, and that means we can't do that. Which one do you want to do?"

We work through that. Everybody has to make some calculated decisions, no matter what your budget is.

The way we're structured, we're going to continue to be really good stewards of what we've got. But like I said, more makes it easier. We're not going to go out and do crazy things that don't make sense because it's about how you build a team.

There are certain teams in our league that have had head coaches in place for a length of time, whether it's Missouri or any of the places that have really built and layered competitive depth, maybe without being the top-resourced programs.

I think you can layer it the right way over time. It's hard to flip it really fast without saying, "Hey, we're going to pour a ton of cash into this and that."

Throwing a bunch of money at it makes it really easy. I think we would have an easy time flipping it even faster.

We're going to get this flipped to where we want it to go, but the timeline gets expedited the more resources you have. But it doesn't guarantee success. You can have more money and still not have the success you want. That's very feasible.

I have no idea, to be honest. I'd love to go to a bowl game in the state of Florida. I love the state of Florida. I'd love to stay in the state for a bowl game. I think it would help with turnout.

When I was the coach at Tulane, we played Florida in the Gasparilla Bowl. Tulane is a smaller private school with great fans and a great turnout.

But I looked around and was like, "All right, are we playing in Ben Hill Griffin Stadium, or are we playing in Tampa?" It was like, "Whoa, this is crazy." It was like playing an SEC road game for us that day.

I'd love to be in-state for a lot of reasons. But I don't spend a whole lot of time worrying about the selection process. I just want to try to win one more game. Then whatever game they put us in, we'll try to win one more after that.

Being in the state of Florida for a bowl game would be great. It would beat having to go somewhere way up north where it's cold.

Finish the season the right way. This game is different. You cannot relax or stop right now. We have to finish strong. I've talked about how you remember a game like this forever. You just do. It's the nature of it, so enjoy it.

I also talked to them about, "Hey, you want to be a professional football player?" Every time I ask the team who wants to play in the NFL, 100% of the hands go up. You get a chance to be in the NFL this week. There's no class. Classes are canceled all week.

That doesn't change anything for us. We only get 20 hours with them. But our players have an opportunity to make a choice to spend more time on their craft, watch more tape and do some things if they want to be great.

You want to be a pro player? Practice being a pro player this week. You don't have school. That's another thing I brought up to all of them because they all think pro football is the next thing. Let's go ahead and act like you're a pro this week.

You have to educate your team on what those games mean because it may not naturally mean to them what it means to our fan base. If you've grown up a Florida Gator, a game like this means the world. For our guys to understand what that means to the fan base is important.

I think it's a little easier when it's an in-state matchup because that's probably more natural.

The out-of-state ones are probably a little bit more unique. We don't play Tennessee anymore on an annual basis. To our transfer guys, they may not understand the history of that type of game.

I think the history of Florida-Florida State probably speaks for itself a little bit more. But we do a good job of trying to educate them on what it means to the state and to Florida people.

I showed them some stuff yesterday about the history of this game. We talked about the series, the most recent history, the long-term history and why bragging rights matter. I'll do a couple more things as the week goes to give them some education on it, more so than motivation.

It's just so they understand what this game is about.

That is something I think is going to be new and unique. Being a college football junkie, bowl games have meant so much to the game, players, pageantry and fans.

I do think, with the 24-team playoff, how does that affect some of the bowls you would consider mid-tier or lower? Obviously, I think that's a concern.

I don't know how that's going to look as we move forward. I haven't spent a ton of time thinking about it, but I have thought about that. That's something that may be lost that has been great about college football for a long time.

To me, it's very helpful. For us this year, being in a bowl game is such a big deal for a lot of young players.

Nowadays, unless you're in the CFP, you've seen more opt-outs in bowl games. You want to win every time you play.

But a bowl game, especially if you get one that is later in December or earlier in January, gives you a longer time to get young players an extended practice session. It's almost like stealing a spring practice's worth of practices. You can get them a whole bunch of reps banked. They can speed up their development and readiness for the next year.

We've had some young players who have flashed at times this year, even if they haven't played a ton of snaps on game day, who are growing. That opportunity would be very helpful for a young player.

I think being around your guys helps because you're more connected and engaged with them through interaction.

When there's distance between you and somebody, it's harder to work through the process of them staying. It creates natural communication. I'm a firm believer that where there's a lack of communication, usually negativity fills that void.

When you're around somebody every day, you can control the narrative of what it's going to look like moving forward, as opposed to having 8,000 people in their ear telling them what it's going to look like moving forward.

Last year, when I took this job, we had just finished the regular season on a Saturday.

The Saturday that the Florida State game was here, I was at Tulane playing UNC Charlotte. Then we played North Texas the next Friday in our conference championship game.

We're using a really similar model. It worked OK then, so I figured let's not deviate too much.

We're using a very similar model to what I did a year ago to prepare our team for the North Texas Friday night championship game. I had a lot more going on then. I was doing this, trying to go back and do that and juggling a lot of things.

But yeah, we're on a similar format schedule-wise.

I think the quarterback is talented athletically. He has played a lot of college football. He's a playmaker, can extend plays, make plays with his legs and make all the throws.

Their receiving corps, to me, is really scary. I think Nos. 8, 7 and 15 are all big-time players. No. 8, Micahi Danzy, has a lot of top-end speed. No. 7, Jasen Lopez, has a basketball background. He's a very natural, efficient mover. No. 15, Jayvan Boggs, has really good ball skills.

I know they've been banged up a little bit at running back. That has been a challenge for them.

Defensively, they've got some athletic dudes. When I turn on the tape, up front, the twins, Nos. 1 and 3, are really good players. They've recruited some athletic guys defensively who stick out. There are a lot of guys where you turn on the tape, watch them individually and they're very impressive.

It's funny. I talked to Stock years ago about Buster before I really knew Buster.

When I took the Troy job — I've made this known — the top two guys I interviewed for my offensive coordinator job were Buster Faulkner and Joe Craddock, who are now both here.

I called Stock during that time. I was in Orlando coaching in the Citrus Bowl with Kentucky. I had already taken the Troy job and was doing both jobs, just like I did here. I don't like it, but I think it's the right thing to do if you can do it.

I'll never forget, I was at Disney Springs with my kids and on the phone with Stock for about half an hour talking about Buster. What's unique is Coach Craddock was also at Middle Tennessee. Stock is an unbelievable football coach. He's a good man. He's a coach's coach.

I think he has done a really good job in a tough circumstance there. Anytime there's an interim midseason change, the timing is difficult.

I think Stock has done a phenomenal job of keeping it together. Those guys are playing really hard. Right when he took over, you could feel on film that there was a little bit of juice and excitement. He's a really good football coach. He did an unbelievable job at Middle Tennessee.

When I took the job at Troy, I studied some of the stuff he had done at Middle because they're similar programs. I had a lot of respect for what he had done.

That place obviously means a lot to him. It's his alma mater, and I know how much respect he has for their program, Coach Bowden and the history of that place. I respect the heck out of Stock. There are some connections there.

I'm not going to declare for him. He's going to make his own decisions.

Our Senior Day policy — and this is what I've done as a head coach — is guys exhausting their eligibility or fourth-year players who are on track to graduate.

We don't make decisions in the middle of the season on third-year guys or guys who aren't in that situation regarding walking.

There are some things I'm going to do to make sure Jadan is honored if this is the end because he needs to be honored here the right way, whether now is the end or not.

I'd like for one of those things to be for him to get 1,000 yards in back-to-back seasons. That would be one of them.

The team always votes on the special-teams captain. The core special-teams players — there's a list of about 18 guys who play a significant role on special teams — vote to make one of their peers a captain each week. That was Toombs last week.

The other guys, myself and the staff pick. Jadan was kind of my pick because he decided to stay when he could've easily left. I wanted him to be honored last week at home if that was his last opportunity to play in The Swamp.

#2565. Mike Breen ‘worried’ about Clyde Frazier after Rockettes comment

The Radio City Rockettes stole the spotlight during the Knicks' 123-116 victory over the Pacers at Madison Square Garden on Sunday.

Members of the famous dance company performed during the first quarter and captured the attention of Knicks legend Walt "Clyde" Frazier, who gushed over them on the MSG Network broadcast with lead play-by-play announcer Mike Breen.

"Hey Alan, if you need any help interviewing the Rockettes, I'm free at halftime," Frazier, a Knicks color analyst, said referring to studio analyst Alan Hahn.

"They're still here, Clyde, if you want to dance with them at halftime," Hahn quipped.

"I could show them the spots around the Garden, you know," replied Frazier, who played 10 seasons with the Knicks, from 1967-77.

"See now this is what I was talking about earlier that I'm worried," Breen said while laughing. "Your filter is starting to come off."

Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle explained that he had an interaction with the Rockettes that caused him to be late to his press conference after the game.

Members of the Radio City Rockettes perform during a game between the Indiana Pacers and the New York Knicks at Madison Square Garden, Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Clyde Frazier and Mike Breen speak before the game between the New Orleans Pelicans and the New York Knicks on Dec. 1, 2024 at Madison Square Garden in New York City, New York. NBAE via Getty Images

"Sorry I was late. The Rockettes were doing a photo shoot in the freight elevator," Carlisle said. "That's what makes New York a special place.

"Luckily there was a ball boy that was able to text somebody and he directed me to a different elevator. I'm appreciative of that."

The Knicks have won five in a row and are in the fourth spot in the Eastern Conference heading into Wednesday's game against the Cavaliers.

#2561. Packers star Micah Parsons reportedly cleared to return from ACL injury, will make season debut vs. Rams on Thanksgiving Eve

The Green Bay Packers star pass rusher has been cleared to return and will make his season debut in their Thanksgiving Eve matchup with the Los Angeles Rams, according to NFL Network's Ian Rapoport.

It will mark Parsons' first NFL game since he tore his left ACL and meniscus last season. He went down with the knee injury in their loss to the Denver Broncos on Dec. 14, and has been sidelined recovering ever since. The Packers lost four straight games after losing Parsons, including a playoff loss to the Chicago Bears.

Parsons had 12.5 sacks in his 14 games with the Packers last season, and had accounted for a third of Green Bay's pressures before he went down. It was his first campaign with the team after they acquired him in a deal with the Dallas Cowboys just ahead of Week 1.

Parsons was cleared to return to practice earlier this month and started his ramp-up while targeting Wednesday's game at SoFi Stadium for his return. Clearly, that has gone exactly to plan.

"I feel like I've done everything I possibly could to put myself in position to potentially play on Wednesday, but for sure this season," Parsons said on Monday afternoon before meeting with team doctors, via The Athletic's Matt Schneidman. "I'll be looking forward to returning to the field."

The Packers will enter Wednesday with a 6-4 record, which has them in last in the NFC North but very much alive in the playoff conversation. They were off last week, but went into their bye fresh off a rough 30-10 loss to the Minnesota Vikings.

While it's been almost a full year since he's played in a game, Parsons is more than ready to get back. And he's well aware of what a win over the Rams would do for the Packers' postseason hopes entering the final stretch of the season.

"I'm really geeked up," he said, via ESPN's Rob Demovsky. "They're like, 'Are you nervous?' I'm just so focused, I'm so locked in on what's ahead of me. I understand what this game Wednesday means to the organization, myself and what it means for playoffs. If I can be out there, I want to be out there."

#2557. Shawn Horcoff Following His Own Plan for the Red Wings

Following the completion of the blockbuster trade sending Dylan Larkin out of Hockeytown, Red Wings interim general manager Shawn Horcoff addressed the media for the first time in nearly a month. Horcoff began his availability with a statement thanking Larkin, his agency, and the team for their patience throughout the process.

The Red Wings, unburdened of the cloud prevailing over the team with Larkin's lingering absence now have much to look forward to as they begin to unpack the trade haul Horcoff netted in return for the former Detroit captain. In the end, the Red Wings sent out Larkin, a 2028 second round pick, and a third round pick for a package centered around a return of top prospect Michael Hage, NHL veterans Kirby Dach and Sam Montembeault and a collection of draft picks over the next three years, including two first round picks.

"In the end, [and] through a long process, Montreal's package was the right one for us. Not just today, but I think in the long term as well," Horcoff said, "And we were able to add a lot of pieces that I think are going to help us accomplish what we want to accomplish long term."

While Horcoff wouldn't define exactly what it was that led to him finally deciding to get the Red Wings over the hump with this trade, one of the main pieces of that was helping to secure a Red Wings future it's still not 100% certain he will be in charge of.

"The opportunity to acquire, a prospect like Michael Hage is, sorry, was, hard for us to pass up. I think the skill in the sense that, and the potential that he brings in a position [center] that is very vital to be successful in the National Hockey League, it was hard to pass up." Horcoff said.

Horcoff and his staff have plenty of familiarity with Hage, as the young Canadian center not plays with Horcoff's son, Will, on the University of Michigan Wolverines, but is his roommate.

Detroit also selected another Michigan forward, JP Hurlbert in the latest NHL entry draft. Hage will certainly help to patch one of the obvious long term roster holes the team has in their top six, and should make the jump to the professional level later this year.

However, in the here and now, the other major pieces of this trade will help to hopefully round out the bottom of the Red Wings depth chart.

"We're just, we're struggling to score some goals right now. So, you know, that's kind of the issue with the guys." Horcoff said.

The package the Red Wings received points more in the direction of a retool, or rather, a soft reset in the timeline of the team's long term rebuild. They'll be hoping that the addition of 25-year-old Dach will help bring more energy, and a better fit, for the team's bottom six and some of the other young forwards in their own system.

"Even the other pieces that we had with, you know, Kirby Dach is a [former] top 3 pick. [He's] a player that's still young, has battled some injuries in the past, but is playing some really good hockey right now."

The addition of Montembeault could very well help the Red Wings steal some games later this year with their depth at goaltender as well. Between him, John Gibson, Daniil Tarasov, and Michal Postava, Detroit has a stable of four NHL quality goalies and could very well decide to flip one or more in the coming months to a team in need.

"I think we're still trying to add around, you know, Moritz or Lucas Raymond. We have a base and a core group of young players that are ready to play in the NHL now, with numerous other ones in Grand Rapids…college players…. Like, these players are ready and and close to playing in the National Hockey League," Horcoff said,

"So I don't view this as a rebuild. I view it more as super exciting about what the next year or 2 is going to hold here. And on top of that, we're going to have some draft capital and cap space to go out there and add some experienced players to help that process along. It's my plan."

Dylan Larkin Excited to Start Canadiens Career

#2556. Is the Rams 2027 draft set to be one of their worst ever?

The Los Angeles Rams are only projected to have one selection in the first 110 picks of the 2027 NFL Draft, and right now that is set at 49th overall. The Rams do not have a first-round pick because of the Myles Garrett trade, and do not have a third-round pick because of the Trent McDuffie trade. In addition, L.A. is not projected to have a single compensatory pick in 2027.

Without a trade and/or getting a third-round comp pick from the NFL's minority hiring incentive program, the Rams will only have one fairly mediocre draft pick until the fourth round. As of Week 12, that is pick 49 in the second round.

Not only are the Cleveland Browns projected to make the playoffs, they also hold L.A.'s first-round pick in 2027, which could end up being in the top-20, if not higher.

The Rams are shutting down players left and right, similar to 2022 when Matthew Stafford, Cooper Kupp and Aaron Donald landed on IR, as both Puka Nacua and Terrance Ferguson have been announced to not be returning this season. Jaylen Watson, whom as a free agency signing may have cost L.A. their only potential 2027 comp pick, is also on season-ending IR. So is Poona Ford. And Davante Adams won't be back soon.

But there is no incentive for the Rams to throw in the towel for a higher draft pick. Coincidentally, there also wasn't any incentive like that in 2022 and the Rams ended up sending pick 6 to the Detroit Lions to complete the Matthew Stafford trade.

The Lions used that pick to trade down and then they selected Jahmyr Gibbs, the frontrunner for Offensive Player of the Year in 2026.

Even after this year, the Rams will still owe the Browns a second-round pick in 2028, and a third-round pick in 2029. The 2029 pick becomes a first-round pick if Garrett is traded to an AFC North team. The 2027 draft will complete L.A.'s trade for McDuffie.

So L.A.'s first pick is projected to be 49th and their second pick is projected to be their original fourth-rounder. Right now that's "113" but that's before we add in compensatory pick. It may actually end up being 120th.

The Rams have their own picks in rounds 5 and 6, but traded their 7th for Tre'Davious White.

So unless the Rams make some trades and/or a team hires Nate Scheelhaase as head coach, L.A. will end up with picks in rounds 2, 4, 5, and 6. This could be their most challenging draft since 2022 when the first pick was Logan Bruss.

Los Angeles avoided going down 0-3 five years ago with a home victory, and if it hopes to keep alive its chances of becoming the first NL team in Major League Baseball history to win three consecutive World Series titles, it likely will need a triumph at Dodger Stadium on Wednesday as it hosts the Milwaukee Brewers at 8 p.m. ET for Game 3 of the 2026 NLCS.

#2554. Grizzlies rookie Cam Boozer out at least 4 weeks with ankle injury

Memphis Grizzlies rookie Cam Boozer is expected to miss at least four weeks with an ankle injury.

The Grizzlies announced news of the injury on Monday. Imaging of the injury revealed a moderate-grade medial sprain of Boozer's right ankle.

Per the Grizzlies, testing also confirmed the structural stability of Boozer's ankle, and he's expected to make a full recovery. He'll be reevaluated in approximately four weeks.

Boozer sustained the injury during Thursday's win over the Warriors. He grabbed his ankle in pain after falling following a drive to the basket. He was sidelined for Sunday's loss to the Timberwolves.

The news is a tough blow for the Grizzlies and Boozer, who's off to a strong start to his NBA career.

Memphis selected Boozer with the No. 2 pick in June's NBA Draft following a freshman season at Duke that earned him consensus National Player of the Year honors. Boozer has so far delivered on his potential as a standout from a rookie class that's projected as one of the best in NBA history.

In 16 games, Boozer's averaged 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds, 5.3 assists, 0.8 steals and 0.8 blocks per game. As with most rookies, Boozer has room to improve his efficiency with a 41.7% field-goal rate and a 32.2% success rate from 3. But his dynamic offensive game as a skilled, floor-stretching power forward has translated to the NBA early in his career.

Boozer's impact has contributed to winning on the floor for Memphis. A Grizzlies team that finished 25-57 last season is off to a 12-5 start, which was good for fifth place in the West going into Monday's games. Boozer has emerged a a leader on the team alongside breakout guard Ty Jerome (24.8 ppg, 5.3 apg) as the Grizzlies look like contenders to return to the postseason.

They'll look to stay afloat in the top half of the Western Conference standings over the next four weeks without their standout rookie.

#2553. Iowa State football star Aiden Flora will return to Cyclones next season

Iowa State football and its fans have one more thing to be thankful for this Thanksgiving.

Former walk-on turned star running back Aiden Flora announced on social media on Nov. 23 that he is returning to Iowa State next season, squashing any worries or concern over a potential departure.

"LOYAL SON FOREVER TRUE!" Flora posted on X. "With God as my guide and my love for the Iowa State football team and Cyclone Nation, I've made the decision to stay home! Go Cyclones and Happy Thanksgiving!"

Flora is relishing a breakout season that has seen him earn national-level success and attention. He currently leads the nation with 1,506 rushing yards. Flora also has 14 rushing touchdowns, a punt return touchdown and has two passing touchdowns. The Adel native is also a semifinalist for the Doak Walker Award, an accolade given to the FBS' top running back in the country, and a finalist for the Paul Hornung Award, which is awarded to the most versatile player in college football.

He has rushed for 170 or more yards and at least one touchdown in each of the last four games for the Cyclones.

Flora's intent to return next season solidifies the Cyclones' backfield, bolsters the offense, and gives a surge of morale and excitement for head coach Jimmy Rogers and the football program. There's still one final regular-season game against Kansas State on Nov. 28, but soon it'll be time for Rogers and his staff to replenish the roster for the 2027 campaign. Having Flora around gives a nice foundation to help build on offensively.

Eugene Rapay covers Iowa State athletics for the Des Moines Register. Contact Eugene at erapay@gannett.com. Follow him on Twitter at @erapay5.

#2552. Report: NFL Primetime documentary is in the works

The recent passing of Tom Jackson has sparked much discussion about ESPN's NFL Primetime, which was for years a mandatory Sunday evening destination for football fans everywhere.

As it turns out, a documentary is coming regarding the show.

Via Sam Neumann of Awful Announcing, the final product is believed to be on track for a release in connection with the Super Bowl, which is being televised by ESPN and ABC.

It could be part of the game-day programming. Three years ago, CBS debuted You Are Looking Live! — a documentary about The NFL Today — before the launch of the official pregame show in advance of Super Bowl LVIII.

For those who were actively following the NFL in the late '80s, NFL Primetime provided the first opportunity to see extended highlights of NFL games on the day they were played. Years before the Internet, social media, Sunday Ticket, and RedZone, Primetime became appointment viewing for fans who wanted to see more about what was happening beyond the two or three games available in their local markets on Sunday afternoon.

And the team of Chris Berman and Tom Jackson became the tour guides for the opportunity to essentially watch all of the important plays from every game in a compressed but meaningful package, with Berman's cadence and the music accompanying the words creating the loose impression that it was all happening live. Even though everyone knew it wasn't.

It became a unique moment in time, which at the time didn't seem to have a shelf life. It's just the way things were, with fans having no concept that there would ever be a way to watch all of the games live or to see any and all relevant plays long before 7:00 p.m. ET. It was a new frontier that no one had reason to believe would not remain a permanent fixture of each and every NFL Sunday.

As Neumann explains it, Jackson recently made a surprise return to ESPN as part of the filming of the documentary. ESPN shared footage of the reunion on Sunday.

It's worth your time to watch it, if you were one of the millions who climbed aboard the Primetime Express every Sunday night of football season for something much more meaningful than a glimpse of the entire day of NFL action. And the documentary will undoubtedly bring back fond memories of the final years before advances in technology caused a narrow range of football options to explode into today's endless menu of choices.

#2551. Brewers bash champion Dodgers to brink of NLCS sweep; CBS Sports Preseason All-America team revealed

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

This is an article version of the CBS Sports HQ AM Newsletter, the ultimate guide to every day in sports. You can sign up to get it in your inbox every weekday morning here.

One of the biggest benefits of the NIL era is that college basketball has more elite talent than ever, now that players are returning for bigger paychecks instead of bolting for NBA money at the first opportunity.

That made selecting our 2026-27 CBS Sports Preseason All-America teams especially difficult, but our experts nailed it. Each of the three teams features players who will be performing at the next level very soon, but the best of those might be Florida superstar Thomas Haugh.

As our own Isaac Trotter explains, if Haugh can improve his range shooting, he won't have to wait long to hear his name called in the 2027 NBA Draft.

When it comes to how the talent broke down by conference, it was a a pretty level playing field for the most part.

Not to spoil anyting, but Arizona freshman Caleb Holt did not make the cut. He did, however, turn some heads in the Wildcats' exhibition game.

Loyalty is an excellent quality to have in a friend or partner. It's also why we love dogs.

It should not be quite as prevalent among college football coaches, some of whom have been undone because they did what was comfortable, not what was necessarily best for the team.

Our own John Talty highlighted five high-profile coaches who have been loyal to a fault, making hires and personnel decisions that have come back to haunt them. One of those coaches is Mike Elko. After a shaky season from Marcel Reed in 2025, Texas A&M chose not to pursue an upgrade in the portal.

Instead, the Aggies gave Reed a major payday, and they'll miss the College Football Playoff because of it.

Look, it can happen to the best of us. Dan Lanning and Dabo Swinney wound up on the list, despite the success that those two have had throughout their careers.

Steve Sarkisian is another coach who has fallen into the trap of being too loyal, but he may have a huge decision on his hands in the offseason. Arch Manning has struggled enough that another year in college should be on the table. If that happens, would Sark welcome back Manning with open arms, despite his shortcomings?

Finally, one person for whom loyalty is no an issue is Missouri QB Austin Simmons. After Trinidad Chambliss won the starting job at Ole Miss last year, Simmons didn't feel the need to stick around, but there won't be bitterness when Simmons returns to Oxford this weekend.
